### Other voices made from the same speech. Outcome (00:08): FAILED

Statement of 25 November, 23:04. The recurrent listener refitted with every cut distorted before its frames are named (bands stretched or squeezed along frequency by up to 15%, a tilt, 100 ms blanked); same 22.75 hours, names, 6,000 updates and tests. Frames named for source speakers kept out: 0.758 against 0.756 (within 1.5 points: HELD). Telling phones apart, stage two, same triplets:

| Test | recurrent listener | with distorted voices | change in the mean |
|---|---|---|---:|
| Spanish: voicing, place, manner, vowel | 0.750, 0.726, 0.716, 0.751 (0.736) | 0.750, 0.713, 0.699, 0.745 (0.727) | −0.9 |
| Italian: the same, then length | 0.696, 0.729, 0.750, 0.737 (0.728); 0.554 | 0.721, 0.731, 0.754, 0.741 (0.737); 0.591 | +0.9 |
| LibriSpeech dev-clean | 0.734, 0.799, 0.863, 0.760 (0.789) | 0.733, 0.788, 0.846, 0.759 (0.782) | −0.7 |

Predicted a rise of 1 to 3 points in both unheard languages: found −0.9 and +0.9, so FAILED, and by the branch frozen beforehand made-up voices are not the lever. Less than 1 point on LibriSpeech: HELD. No family at 0.90: HELD. The one cell that moved by more than its interval is Italian voicing (+2.5).

Standing of the first target after five listeners on the same 22.8 hours (convolutional, recurrent, with a pair loss, without names, with distorted voices): every one that was taught names lies within 3 points of the others in mean, 0.73 to 0.79, and 11 to 17 points under the bar. Network shape, loss and made-up voices each moved it by about a point; eleven times the speech moved it by 3 to 8. Looked up after the miss: what is reported to keep and sharpen a language's contrasts on this kind of test is learning from hundreds to thousands of hours without names by predicting masked or coming stretches (wav2vec 2.0, HuBERT, contrastive predictive coding); a search summary says so for all three, and I did not open the papers behind it. Such a model fitted on 53 languages names phones in unseen languages (Xu, Baevski and Auli, Simple and effective zero-shot cross-lingual phoneme recognition, arxiv.org/abs/2109.11680). That such frames score in the 0.9s across speakers on the published tests is from memory, not checked tonight. It is two to three orders of magnitude more speech than is on this disk, and a listener fitted elsewhere on such speech is a decision about what counts as discovery under the goal, which is the user's to make. The 0.90 in every family is reported as unreached.

### The installed listener on the families it was not tested on. Statement (00:11)

The goal check found the curriculum unfinished: the installed listener was scored on voicing, place, manner, vowel quality and Italian consonant length, and not on vowel length, aspiration, nasality, ejectives or implosives. Recordings for these are on disk from the earlier work: the audited phone intervals of the UCLA archive in 95 languages (VoxAngeles, cut by hand, CC BY-NC), none of which the listener was fitted on. Each word recording is heard whole by the installed package and the audited interval is cut from the frames.

Protocol, frozen before any score is seen. A and B are the plain and the marked form of one base sound (a and aː, t and tʰ, a and ã, k and kʼ, b and ɓ) from one language; X is one of the two forms from another language, so another voice and another language at once. Neighbouring sounds are not matched. Intervals of at least 30 ms. For each base sound 1,000 triplets a direction, drawn by language pair and then by token; a base sound counts with at least two languages for A and B and 200 distinct triplets; a family's score is the mean over its base sounds, its interval from resampling X's languages. Compared by the package's own comparison (which removes duration), by the same comparison on frequency bands, and by duration alone. The four families already tested are scored the same way as a yardstick for how hard this cross-language form is.

Predictions. Vowel length and consonant length: the listener at most 0.65, duration alone at least 0.70 and above the listener (chance 0.7). Aspiration: the listener 0.60 to 0.72 and above the bands (0.5); it was never taught the mark. Nasality: 0.58 to 0.70 (0.5); it was taught French nasal vowels. Ejectives and implosives: too few tokens for most base sounds to count (0.6). The four yardstick families: 0.65 to 0.78 in mean, under their Spanish and Italian scores since context is free (0.6). No family at 0.90 (0.9).

Outcome (00:13). 5,439 word recordings in 95 languages, heard through the installed package in 23 s. Cross-language ABX on hand-cut intervals, 1,000 triplets a direction for each base sound, intervals over X's languages:

| Family | base sounds counted | the listener | frequency bands | duration alone |
|---|---:|---|---:|---:|
| voicing (yardstick) | 5 | 0.767 [0.752, 0.783] | 0.683 | 0.528 |
| place (yardstick) | 4 | 0.705 [0.689, 0.722] | 0.539 | 0.516 |
| manner (yardstick) | 4 | 0.804 [0.792, 0.817] | 0.677 | 0.520 |
| vowel quality (yardstick) | 5 | 0.706 [0.685, 0.713] | 0.579 | 0.511 |
| vowel length | 16 of 35 | 0.510 [0.486, 0.531] | 0.472 | 0.586 |
| consonant length | 21 of 63 | 0.496 [0.456, 0.530] | 0.480 | 0.698 |
| aspiration | 11 of 26 | 0.532 [0.504, 0.572] | 0.493 | 0.490 |
| nasality | 10 of 17 | 0.541 [0.516, 0.555] | 0.507 | 0.504 |
| ejectives | 4 of 12 | 0.519 [0.483, 0.577] | 0.482 | 0.524 |
| implosives | 2 of 2 | 0.503 [0.457, 0.564] | 0.506 | 0.475 |

Against the predictions. Length: the listener at most 0.65, HELD (0.51, 0.50, chance); duration alone at least 0.70, FAILED for vowels (0.586) and by a hair for consonants (0.698), though it is above the listener for both. Aspiration 0.60 to 0.72: FAILED, 0.532. Nasality 0.58 to 0.70: FAILED, 0.541; being taught French nasal vowels by name did not make nasality a direction its frames keep across languages. Ejectives too few to count for most base sounds: HELD (4 of 12, and 2 to 3 languages each); implosives counted after all, at chance. Yardstick mean 0.65 to 0.78: HELD at 0.746, but not under the Spanish and Italian scores as predicted (0.736, 0.728): with any neighbours and another language for X, hand-cut intervals score as high as corpus-aligned ones with the next phone matched. No family at 0.90: HELD.

Reading. The installed listener hears which phone it is, in languages it never met, and nothing that the six source lexicons do not write as a different phone: length, aspiration, nasality, glottalic release. These families are tested and none advances. The curriculum's first four families stand at 0.71 to 0.80 across 95 languages; the next five stand at chance with this listener. Two things follow to try: for length, duration measured against the rest of the word, since the word is there to measure against; for the others, a listener taught the marks, which the earlier work on this corpus did with labelled pairs from other languages (0.75 for aspiration, 0.72 for nasality in order, under its own protocol).

### Length against the rest of the word. Statement (00:13)

One addition to the same test: beside duration alone, the interval's duration divided by the mean duration of the other intervals of its word, which takes out how fast that speaker said that word. A token alone in its word counts as a coin toss. Predictions: vowel length rises from 0.586 to at least 0.65 (chance 0.5), consonant length from 0.698 to at least 0.75 (0.5); the yardstick families stay within 3 points of 0.50 (0.8).

Outcome (00:14): FAILED. Duration against the word scores 0.586 for vowel length (0.585 alone) and 0.656 for consonant length (0.698 alone); the yardstick families stay at 0.51 to 0.53 (HELD). Taking out the word's pace does not help and for consonants it hurts. What one language writes as long is not a fixed multiple of its short sound that carries to another language; with A and B from one language and X from another, a single pair of examples does not place X. Length is a convention inside a language, and the place to test it is across speakers of one language with a few examples, which is the convention test (Italian consonant length 0.72 with the duration reader) and not a one-shot comparison across languages.

### Pitch as a second thing heard. Statement (00:16)

Idea. The listener's frames are built to say which phone it is and they normalise each band, so the melody of a syllable is not in them. Pitch is a transparent measurement that can stand beside them: for every 10 ms the fundamental frequency from the autocorrelation of a 40 ms window (60 to 400 Hz, the window's own autocorrelation divided out), kept where the peak is at least 0.5 and the frame is not near silence, in octaves against the median of the stretch given, so that a high and a low voice give the same melody. Two stretches are compared by their melodies drawn out to sixteen points each: the mean absolute difference in octaves.

Test. Mandarin single syllables on disk from the earlier work (the Tone Perfect recordings, 508 clips, six voices, four tones). A and B are one syllable in two tones from one voice; X is the same syllable in one of the two tones from another voice. All distinct triplets; a tone pair counts with at least 100; the family is the mean over tone pairs, its interval from resampling syllables. Compared by melody, by the installed listener's frames and by frequency bands.

Predictions: melody at least 0.88 in mean (chance 0.6) and at least 0.80 for every counted tone pair (0.5); the listener's frames at most 0.65 (0.6) and the bands at most 0.65 (0.6). Whether melody reaches 0.90 in mean, the bar for this family: 0.45.

Outcome (00:17): FAILED on three of four. 508 clips, six voices; 68% of frames have a pitch. Tone pairs with at least 100 distinct triplets: 1/3 (128), 1/4 (1,144), 2/4 (288).

| Compared by | mean of counted pairs | 1/3 | 1/4 | 2/4 | uncounted: 1/2 (68), 2/3 (24), 3/4 (20) |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| melody | 0.810 [0.769, 0.833] | 0.609 | 0.866 | 0.955 | 0.868, 0.917, 0.750 |
| the listener's frames | 0.635 [0.563, 0.672] | 0.531 | 0.636 | 0.736 | 0.691, 0.625, 0.550 |
| frequency bands | 0.752 [0.652, 0.785] | 0.906 | 0.622 | 0.729 | 0.706, 0.500, 0.850 |

Melody at least 0.88: FAILED (0.810), and not every pair at 0.80 (the high level tone against the low dipping one, 0.609). The listener's frames at most 0.65: HELD (0.635). Bands at most 0.65: FAILED (0.752), because they tell the first tone from the third at 0.906, where melody fails. The third tone said alone is low and often creaky; a creaky stretch has no steady period, so the pitch is lost or halves there, while the creak itself shows in the spectrum. And the melody is taken against the clip's own median, which throws away that the first tone is high and the third low in the speaker's range. Melody is the best single measurement for 1/4 and 2/4 and the worst for 1/3; neither measurement alone covers the family, which is at 0.81 against a bar of 0.90.

### Melody and spectrum together. Statement (00:17)

The rule is fixed before its score is seen, but on the same clips whose single scores are known, so this is an exploration and not a confirmation. For a triplet each measurement says how much nearer X is to its own tone than to the other, as (far − near) / (far + near), which lies between −1 and 1 whatever the measurement's units; the measurements' numbers are added and X goes where the sum points. No weights, nothing fitted. Two sums: melody with bands; melody with bands and the listener's frames. Predictions: melody with bands at least 0.88 in mean of the three counted pairs (chance 0.5), with every counted pair at least 0.80 (0.5); adding the listener's frames changes the mean by under 2 points (0.6).

Outcome (00:18): FAILED, narrowly. Melody with bands: 0.871 [0.778, 0.896] in mean; 1/3 0.766, 1/4 0.886, 2/4 0.962 (0.88 and every pair at 0.80 predicted). With the listener's frames added: 0.882 [0.775, 0.901]; 1/3 0.805, 1/4 0.892, 2/4 0.948; a change of 1.1 points (under 2: HELD). Adding what the spectrum knows to what the pitch knows lifts the family from 0.810 to 0.87 to 0.88 without a fitted number, and every counted pair is then at 0.80 or above only with all three. Pitch and tone stands just under the bar, on single syllables read in isolation by six voices; tone in running speech is not tested.

### Back to the first target: several listeners heard together, and the same voice as a ceiling. Statement (00:18)

Two measurements on the stage-two test, same triplets as before.

(a) The sum that helped for tone, applied to listeners: for each triplet every listener says how much nearer X is to its own phone, (far − near) / (far + near), and the numbers are added. Four listeners taught the same names on the same 22.8 hours but differing in shape, loss or distortion (recurrent, convolutional, with the pair loss, with distorted voices). Their errors are partly their own, so the sum should beat each. Prediction: the mean of the four families rises by 1.5 to 3 points over the best single listener in each of Spanish, Italian and LibriSpeech (chance 0.5); no family reaches 0.90 (0.85).

(b) A ceiling: the installed listener with X taken from the same speaker as A and B, another token. If two tokens of one phone from one voice are not told from a neighbouring phone nine times in ten, no amount of sameness across speakers reaches the bar on these tokens, and the limit is in the tokens (short, cut by a forced alignment, said in running speech) or in the frames' fineness. Prediction: the mean of the four families lies at 0.80 to 0.88 with the same voice in Spanish and LibriSpeech (0.5), that is, 5 to 12 points above the cross-speaker score; under 0.90 in every family (0.6).

### A tone convention through the package's reader. Statement (00:29)

The package now measures melody (operation intone) and its reader has a fifth reader that goes by melody alone, chosen as the duration reader is: only when it accounts for what was pointed at with at least two fewer mistakes than the whole comparison. Version 0.5.0, 21 tests.

Test, the decisive experiment for a suprasegmental. Mandarin syllables crossing a consonant with a tone (cai1, sai1, cai4, sai4 and the like), every set for which at least four voices have all four syllables. The same recordings are taught under two conventions: the tone decides what is pointed at, or the consonant does. Half the voices teach, one example a syllable each; the other half are probed; every split of the voices into halves. Two readers: the package's reader given the heard frames only, and given the melody of every stretch as well.

Predictions. Where the consonant decides: at least 0.90 in mean for both readers (chance 0.6), and giving the melody costs under 2 points (0.7). Where the tone decides: at most 0.70 with frames only (0.6) and at least 0.85 with melody (0.6). With melody, at least three quarters of the sets reach 0.80 under both conventions (0.5).

Outcome (00:32). Fourteen sets (ten with six voices, four with four), 2,592 probes for each reader and convention, every hearing and reading by the package's functions.

| Reader | the consonant decides | the tone decides | sets at 0.80 under both |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| heard frames only | 0.917 | 0.752 | 5 of 14 |
| heard frames and melody | 0.917 | 0.917 | 13 of 14 |

By set with melody, consonant / tone: cai, sai 1/4 0.900 / 0.912; can, san 2/4 1.000 / 0.833; cang, sang 1/4 0.979 / 0.946; cao, sao 2/4 0.938 / 0.917; cen, sen 1/4 0.946 / 0.925; ceng, seng 1/4 0.958 / 0.950; cong, song 1/4 0.963 / 0.929; cou, sou 1/4 0.938 / 0.912; cu, su 1/4 0.863 / 1.000; cuo, suo 1/4 0.896 / 0.879; ji, xi 1/3 0.842 / 0.883; jian, xian 2/4 0.979 / 0.833; jiao, xiao 2/4 0.854 / 0.979; jin, xin 1/4 0.779 / 0.938. The one set that misses does so on the consonant (j against x before in), not on the tone.

Against the predictions. The consonant decides, at least 0.90 for both: HELD. Melody costs under 2 points there: HELD, it costs nothing, since the melody reader is not chosen when the pointing does not follow melody. The tone decides, at most 0.70 with frames only: FAILED, 0.752; duration and what the spectrum keeps of a falling voice carry a quarter of the way. At least 0.85 with melody: HELD, 0.917. Three quarters of the sets at 0.80 under both: HELD, 13 of 14.

So the convention test's bar (0.80 on both members) is met for tone in Mandarin, a language none of the fitting touched, with three examples a syllable from three voices and three other voices probed. Limits: six voices, syllables read alone; with one teacher's answers flipped the same melody decides or does not, but tone in running speech, where neighbouring tones bend each other, is untested.

### Stress: loudness as a third thing heard. Statement (00:35)

Idea. Which syllable of a word is stressed shows in where the word is loud, long and high, not in which phones it has. The package's melody gets a third column, the loudness of each 10 ms frame in bels below the loudest frame of the stretch, and the reader a sixth reader comparing two stretches' loudness drawn out to sixteen points, chosen like the duration and melody readers.

Test. English two-syllable words cut from LibriSpeech by the corpus's word times, with the pronunciation inventory's mark for stress on the first or the second syllable (the earlier work's panels; no vowel intervals are given to the reader). Teaching words from 40 dev-clean speakers, 16 words of each pattern, 20 draws; probes are the 226 words of 33 test-other speakers, other people and almost all other words (test-clean stays unopened). The reader is given frames only, frames with pitch, and frames with pitch and loudness. Chance is 0.50.

Predictions: frames only at most 0.60 (chance 0.6); with pitch 0.58 to 0.72 (0.5); with loudness as well at least 0.72 (0.5) and under 0.90 (0.85). The Mandarin tone conventions, rerun with the sixth reader present, move by under 1 point in either convention (0.7).

### Several listeners together, and the same voice. Outcome (00:36), for the statement of 00:18

(a) Four listeners added, mean of the four families (voicing, place, manner, vowel), stage two, same triplets:

| Test | best single listener | the four together | gain |
|---|---|---|---:|
| Spanish | 0.768 (pair loss) | 0.787: 0.799, 0.776, 0.779, 0.794 | +2.0 |
| Italian | 0.759 (convolutional) | 0.780: 0.739, 0.782, 0.804, 0.793; length 0.585 | +2.0 |
| LibriSpeech dev-clean | 0.800 (pair loss) | 0.822: 0.768, 0.842, 0.891, 0.786 | +2.2 |

A gain of 1.5 to 3 points in each: HELD. No family at 0.90: HELD, the nearest being manner on LibriSpeech at 0.891. The sum over the installed listener alone reproduces its single scores exactly, so the batched comparison used here and the earlier one agree.

(b) The installed listener with X from the same speaker as A and B: Spanish 0.892, 0.877, 0.884, 0.874 (mean 0.882); Italian 0.817, 0.831, 0.841, 0.813 (0.826), length 0.686; LibriSpeech 0.853, 0.888, 0.914, 0.850 (0.876). Predicted 0.80 to 0.88 in Spanish and LibriSpeech: HELD for LibriSpeech and at the edge for Spanish (0.882). Predicted 5 to 12 points above the cross-speaker score: Spanish +14.6 (FAILED, more), Italian +9.8, LibriSpeech +8.7. Under 0.90 in every family: FAILED by one cell, manner on LibriSpeech at 0.914.

Reading. With no change of speaker at all, one token of each phone still places a third token wrongly one time in eight to six: 0.83 to 0.88. A change of speaker costs a further 9 to 15 points, of which listening with four listeners at once gives back 2. So this listener, were every voice the same voice to it, would stand at about 0.88 on these tokens, under the bar. The remaining errors are in the tokens and the one-shot form of the test: phones of 30 to 80 ms in running speech, cut by a forced alignment, with the sound before them free, compared against a single example of each kind. That part of the gap closes with more examples a kind (the convention test, 0.82 to 0.99 at eight examples) and not with a better single comparison; it is why the same listener passes the convention target and misses this one.

Outcome (00:41), stress: FAILED on the main prediction. 358 teaching words, 226 probes from 33 other speakers, 20 draws of 16 words a pattern, read by the package's reader:

| Reader given | right | different vowels (192) | the same vowel (34) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| heard frames only | 0.619 | 0.632 | 0.546 |
| frames and pitch | 0.612 | 0.624 | 0.541 |
| frames, pitch and loudness | 0.653 | 0.658 | 0.625 |

Frames only at most 0.60: FAILED by two points (0.619; the duration reader and the vowels themselves carry a little). With pitch 0.58 to 0.72: HELD, and pitch adds nothing. With loudness at least 0.72: FAILED, 0.653; under 0.90: HELD. Loudness is worth 4.1 points over pitch alone [1.3, 7.6 over draws], better in 8 draws, worse in 1, the same in 11 where its reader was not chosen. The Mandarin conventions with the sixth reader present: 0.9155 and 0.9155 (0.917 and 0.917 before), 13 of 14 sets as before: HELD.

Reading. Comparing whole words by where they are loud and high says little about which syllable is stressed when every word has other sounds: a loud vowel and a stressed vowel are not told apart by a contour. The earlier work on these words compared the two vowels of each word directly and got 0.79 to 0.91 with the vowels pointed out, and 0.82 from level alone; that is a measurement inside the word, which this reader does not make. Stress is tested through this package and does not advance: 0.65 against a chance of 0.50. Kept: the loudness column, for its small steady gain at no cost elsewhere.

### Clicks and ejectives through the installed listener. Statement (00:43)

The earlier work's frozen plan on natural narratives (DoReCo; six Nǁng and five Sanzhi Dargwa speakers; intervals and labels from the corpus): A and B from one speaker, two click places or a plain and an ejective stop, with the same following vowel; C and D the same two kinds from another speaker. A decision is right when C lies nearer A than B, and when D lies nearer B than A. The earlier readers scored 50 to 60% (a codebook), up to 73% (the spectrum of an 8 ms burst). Here each token is heard by the installed listener inside one second of its recording, and compared over the corpus interval, and over the interval with the next 30 ms; frequency bands likewise. Each ordered pair of speakers weighs the same.

Predictions: the listener at most 0.60 on each of the four contrasts in both cuts (chance 0.7); never above the bands by 5 points or more on the clicks, which no source language has (0.6); nothing at 0.90 (0.95).

Outcome (00:44). Share of decisions right, corpus interval / with the next 30 ms, ordered speaker pairs weighing the same:

| Contrast | vowel across speakers | exams | the listener | frequency bands |
|---|---|---:|---|---|
| Nǁng dental / lateral click | same | 534 | 0.540 / 0.522 | 0.505 / 0.490 |
| | changed | 400 | 0.545 / 0.535 | 0.544 / 0.590 |
| Nǁng alveolar / palatal click | same | 438 | 0.615 / 0.575 | 0.538 / 0.524 |
| | changed | 156 | 0.609 / 0.546 | 0.526 / 0.597 |
| Sanzhi k / kʼ | same | 1,576 | 0.532 / 0.549 | 0.631 / 0.590 |
| | changed | 2,380 | 0.560 / 0.541 | 0.624 / 0.554 |
| Sanzhi q / qʼ | same | 1,890 | 0.653 / 0.640 | 0.701 / 0.682 |
| | changed | 5,220 | 0.636 / 0.609 | 0.645 / 0.639 |

At most 0.60 everywhere: FAILED in six of sixteen cells, the highest 0.653 (q against qʼ), so the listener hears a little more of these than predicted and nowhere much. Never 5 points above the bands on clicks: FAILED in one cell (alveolar against palatal, same vowel, 0.615 against 0.538). Nothing at 0.90: HELD. For the ejectives the plain bands are level with the listener or ahead of it. Clicks and ejectives are tested on natural recordings and do not advance: 0.52 to 0.65, with five or six speakers a language.

### Intonation on natural sentences. Statement (00:46)

Natural recordings for intonation are on disk after all: the Common Voice shards the alignments came from carry each sentence's text, and 311 of 3,345 Catalan sentences end in a question mark (Kinyarwanda 87, Bashkir 77, Italian 33). The mark is used only as the teacher's pointing and for scoring; the reader gets sound. Limits known beforehand: a question mark covers questions with a question word, which often fall, as well as those marked by melody alone, and Catalan is one of the listener's source languages, though nothing was ever fitted to melody.

Test. For each language with at least 60 questions (Catalan, Kinyarwanda, Bashkir), every question and as many statements, drawn from the same speakers where they have any. The last second of sound of each sentence is heard. Speakers are halved; sixteen questions and sixteen statements from one half are pointed at, the other half's sentences are read; twenty draws. The package's reader is given frames only, or frames with melody. Beside it one transparent number alone: the pitch of the last 100 ms that has a pitch minus the pitch of the 300 ms before, a question being a rise (above the median of the taught sentences).

Predictions: frames only at most 0.58 in every language (chance 0.6); with melody 0.58 to 0.72 in Catalan (0.5); the final rise alone 0.55 to 0.70 in Catalan (0.5); nothing at 0.80 (0.8).

Outcome (00:51). Catalan: 309 questions and 309 statements from 237 speakers, twenty halvings of the speakers. Kinyarwanda has two speakers with questions and Bashkir one, so neither can be split into people who teach and people who are read; Kinyarwanda's numbers (0.54, 0.52, 0.48 over its one possible halving and its mirror) say nothing, and Bashkir was not run.

| Catalan, question or statement, balanced | right |
|---|---:|
| the reader given heard frames only | 0.500 |
| the reader given frames and melody | 0.564 |
| the final rise alone | 0.659 |

The last 100 ms with a pitch lie 0.136 octaves above the 300 ms before them in questions and 0.113 below in statements. Frames only at most 0.58: HELD (0.500). With melody 0.58 to 0.72: FAILED, 0.564. The final rise alone 0.55 to 0.70: HELD. Nothing at 0.80: HELD.

Reading. The melody carries the difference (a quarter of an octave between the two kinds at the end of the sentence), and one number taken at the right place reads it better than the reader's comparison of the whole last second drawn out to sixteen points, in which the end is one sixteenth. As with stress, the measurement is there and the reader does not yet look in the right place. A question mark also covers questions that fall. Intonation is tested on natural speech in one language and does not advance: 0.66 at best.

### Controlled synthesis for the package's own measurements. Statement (00:52)

The coverage table has had no synthesis of its own for this package. A controlled test isolates one property and holds the rest fixed, which natural recordings cannot. Twelve made voices, each a harmonic source through formant resonances, differing in pitch (95 to 240 Hz), vocal tract length (formants scaled 0.87 to 1.18), spectral tilt and speaking rate (0.85 to 1.2). Seven contrasts, each changing one thing: vowel quality (five formant patterns, the pairs i/e, e/a, a/o, o/u, i/u); voicing (a hiss with and without a low voice bar, s and z); place (the hiss centred at 3 or at 5.5 kHz); length (a vowel of 80 or 160 ms at the voice's rate); aspiration (20 or 80 ms of breath between a burst and its vowel); pitch direction (a third of an octave up or down, for tone and for intonation); stress (two vowels with the first or the second 6 dB louder). A and B from one voice, X from another, 600 triplets a contrast, every token rendered afresh. Compared by the listener's frames, by frequency bands, and by the package's duration, pitch and loudness comparisons.

Predictions: by the listener's frames, vowel quality at least 0.90 (chance 0.6), place at least 0.90 (0.6), voicing at least 0.85 (0.5), aspiration 0.60 to 0.85 (0.4), and length, pitch direction and stress at most 0.65 each (0.6). By the transparent measurements: length by duration at least 0.90 (0.8), pitch direction by pitch at least 0.95 (0.8), stress by loudness at least 0.90 (0.7).

Outcome (00:54): FAILED where it matters most, and for a reason worth more than the scores. Share right, 600 triplets a contrast:

| Contrast | the listener's frames | frequency bands | duration | pitch | loudness |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| vowel quality | 0.470 | 0.508 | 0.500 | 0.499 | 0.567 |
| voicing | 0.735 | 0.762 | 0.500 | 0.261 | 0.810 |
| place | 0.598 | 0.668 | 0.500 | 0.500 | 0.520 |
| length | 0.812 | 0.507 | 1.000 | 0.507 | 0.935 |
| aspiration | 0.943 | 0.708 | 0.902 | 0.496 | 1.000 |
| pitch direction | 0.725 | 0.918 | 0.500 | 1.000 | 0.737 |
| stress | 0.848 | 1.000 | 0.500 | 0.502 | 1.000 |

The transparent measurements do what they were built for: length by duration 1.000, pitch direction by pitch 1.000, stress by loudness 1.000 (all three HELD). The listener's frames fail on the plainest contrast there is, two steady vowels (0.470 where at least 0.90 was predicted), and on place (0.598); voicing 0.735 (0.85 predicted). The cause is in the front end, not in the made voices: every band is given zero mean and unit variance over the stretch that is heard, and a steady sound heard alone is the same in every frame, so the normalisation removes exactly what distinguishes it and leaves the noise. Natural tests never showed this because a token was always heard inside its utterance or was a syllable with a beginning and an end. Where the contrast lies in how the sound changes over time (aspiration 0.943, stress 0.848, length 0.812), the frames keep it. Two more things seen: the frames were predicted blind to length, pitch direction and stress (at most 0.65) and are not (0.81, 0.73, 0.85) when the token is the whole stretch; and the pitch comparison scores 0.261 on voicing because a stretch without a pitch counts as "nothing known" and so as nearest to everything.

A limit to state in the package: a sound must be heard in context. The fair controlled test places each token in a fixed carrier said by the same voice, which is the next statement.

### The same contrasts inside a carrier. Statement (00:54)

One change: every token is placed in a carrier said by its own voice (the vowel o, a hiss at 4.2 kHz, the token, the vowel u, with 40 ms of quiet between), the whole is heard and measured, and the token's rows are cut out by time. Voices, contrasts, triplets and comparisons as before. Predictions: by the listener's frames, vowel quality at least 0.90 (chance 0.6), place at least 0.85 (0.5), voicing at least 0.85 (0.5), aspiration at least 0.85 (0.6); length by duration 1.00, pitch direction by pitch at least 0.95, stress by loudness at least 0.90 (0.8 each).

Outcome (00:57). Inside the carrier, 600 triplets a contrast:

| Contrast | the listener's frames | frequency bands | duration | pitch | loudness |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| vowel quality | 0.920 | 0.953 | 0.500 | 0.522 | 0.658 |
| voicing | 0.905 | 1.000 | 0.500 | 0.263 | 0.603 |
| place | 1.000 | 1.000 | 0.500 | 0.500 | 0.545 |
| length | 0.705 | 0.368 | 0.972 | 0.493 | 0.742 |
| aspiration | 0.907 | 0.822 | 0.933 | 0.492 | 1.000 |
| pitch direction | 0.537 | 0.775 | 0.500 | 0.990 | 0.627 |
| stress | 0.823 | 0.953 | 0.500 | 0.492 | 1.000 |

HELD: vowel quality 0.920, place 1.000, voicing 0.905 and aspiration 0.907 by the listener's frames; pitch direction by pitch 0.990; stress by loudness 1.000. FAILED by a little: length by duration 0.972 where 1.00 was predicted, since the token's rows are cut by time and a row at an edge falls in or out. On made voices that differ in pitch, tract length, tilt and rate, with one property changed and a carrier around the token, every contrast is told apart nine times in ten or better by the measurement meant for it. The bands do as well as the listener or better here: these voices differ in ways a spectrum can undo, and real voices do not, which is where the listener earns its place (0.60 to 0.66 for bands against 0.72 to 0.79 on natural speech). Controlled and natural are therefore reported apart: controlled 0.90 to 1.00, natural 0.70 to 0.86.

Manner is missing from the made contrasts; it is added next with its own prediction: a stop (40 ms of closure, a burst and 20 ms of hiss) against a fricative (120 ms of the same hiss), in the carrier, at least 0.90 by the listener's frames (chance 0.6).

Outcome (00:57), manner: HELD at 1.000 by the listener's frames, and also by bands, duration and loudness; this made pair differs in length as well as in kind, so it is the easiest of the eight and says least.

### Naming a tone in IPA. Statement (00:57)

The naming tests so far covered phones and two marks; no corpus used wrote a suprasegmental. Mandarin's four tones have IPA tone letters (˥, ˧˥, ˨˩˦, ˥˩) and the recordings name each clip's tone. A reading layer for them from a documented amount of labelled sound: eight clips of each tone, 32 clips of about half a second, from three of the six voices, any syllables; the package's reader with four things to choose among. Every clip of the other three voices is then named, twenty halvings of the voices. Scored as the mean over the four tones of the share named rightly (chance 0.25), for the reader given frames only and given the melody as well.

Predictions: with melody at least 0.70 (chance 0.5); frames only at most 0.45 (0.6); the third tone is the worst named with melody (0.6), as it was the worst told apart.

Outcome (01:00). 508 clips, six voices; 32 labelled clips, 23.7 seconds of sound on average, from three voices; the other three voices' clips named, twenty halvings. Mean over the four tones of the share named rightly:

| Reader given | all four | ˥ | ˧˥ | ˨˩˦ | ˥˩ |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| heard frames only | 0.396 | 0.307 | 0.358 | 0.537 | 0.383 |
| frames and melody | 0.919 | 0.999 | 0.942 | 0.798 | 0.939 |

With melody at least 0.70: HELD, 0.919. Frames only at most 0.45: HELD. The third tone worst: HELD, 0.798; it is named as the second 9% of the time and as the first 8%. So the one suprasegmental a corpus on disk writes can be named in IPA from under half a minute of labelled sound, by the melody measurement and not by the frames. These are syllables read alone; tone letters in running speech are untested.

### Careful speech before connected speech. Statement (01:01)

The goal's progression starts from plain cases and moves to new contexts and connected speech; the first target was measured only at the hard end (phones of 30 to 80 ms in read sentences, cut by an alignment). The Mandarin syllables are natural speech at the plain end: one syllable read alone, six voices, cut by its own silence. Pairs of syllables that differ in one segment and share the rest and the tone: manner (the initials c and s, j and x), place (the final n and ng: an and ang, en and eng, in and ing, ian and iang), vowel quality (a and e before n and before ng, a and o before ng, ai and ao, ou and uo, u and ou, u and uo). There is no voicing pair with enough voices. A and B are the two syllables from one voice, X one of them from another voice; all distinct triplets; a pair counts with at least 50; whole syllables compared by the installed listener's frames, heard alone as recorded, and by frequency bands.

Predictions: manner at least 0.85 (chance 0.5); place 0.65 to 0.85 (0.5); vowel quality at least 0.85 (0.5); at least one family at 0.90 (0.5); the listener above the bands in all three (0.7).

Outcome (01:02): FAILED. Whole syllables read alone, across six voices, by the installed listener's frames (frequency bands in brackets):

| Family | mean of counted pairs | pairs (triplets) |
|---|---|---|
| manner | 0.759 (0.638) | c/s 0.772 (1,056); j/x 0.747 (564) |
| place | 0.768 (0.710) | an/ang 0.641 (64); en/eng 0.750 (240); in/ing 0.716 (88); ian/iang 0.964 (56) |
| vowel quality | 0.692 (0.737) | an/en 0.594; ang/eng 0.558; ang/ong 0.708; ai/ao 0.641; ou/uo 0.840; u/ou 0.683; u/uo 0.817 |

Manner at least 0.85: FAILED (0.759). Place 0.65 to 0.85: HELD (0.768). Vowel quality at least 0.85: FAILED (0.692). One family at 0.90: FAILED. The listener above the bands in all three: FAILED, the bands lead on vowels. Careful speech is not the easier stage under this comparison: two whole syllables that share all but one segment are mostly alike, and the mean over the alignment spreads the one difference over everything they share, the same dilution seen on three-phone stretches on 25 November; and a syllable heard alone meets the normalisation weakness found tonight. The plain-end stage does not rescue the first target: on natural speech it stands at 0.69 to 0.77 here, 0.70 to 0.86 in read sentences, 0.71 to 0.80 across 95 languages; only the made voices reach 0.90.

### The flagship question asked across eight ways of hearing. Statement (01:03)

Does hearing a difference better make a language's use of it quicker to learn? So far this was answered with two points, the bands and the installed listener. There are eight ways of hearing on disk that differ in how well they tell phones apart across speakers in Spanish (mean of four families): frequency bands 0.604, the label-free listener 0.608, the convolutional listener on 1.9 hours 0.688, the recurrent one on 2.0 hours 0.700, with distorted voices 0.727, the installed one 0.736, the convolutional on all hours 0.764, with the pair loss 0.768. The convention test (Spanish, six sets, the lower of the two conventions, 1, 2, 4 and 8 examples a syllable, same probes and histories for every arm) is run for the six not yet run.

Predictions: over the eight, the rank correlation between telling apart and following the convention at four examples a syllable is at least 0.8 (chance 0.6); the label-free listener, which tells apart no better than the bands, follows conventions within 3 points of the bands (0.6); every listener that tells apart at 0.69 or better reaches 0.80 under both conventions in more sets than the bands do, which is none (0.8).

Outcome (01:06). Spanish, six sets, the lower of the two conventions, mean over sets; sets at 0.80 under both conventions within eight examples a syllable:

| Way of hearing | tells apart (ABX) | 1 example | 2 | 4 | 8 | sets at 0.80 | examples needed by set |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| frequency bands | 0.604 | 0.576 | 0.619 | 0.672 | 0.704 | 0 | never |
| label-free listener | 0.608 | 0.600 | 0.637 | 0.677 | 0.711 | 1 | 8 in one set |
| convolutional, 1.9 h of names | 0.688 | 0.677 | 0.725 | 0.776 | 0.806 | 4 | 8, 8, 4, 4 |
| recurrent, 2.0 h of names | 0.700 | 0.685 | 0.748 | 0.795 | 0.822 | 4 | 8, 4, 2, 4 |
| recurrent, distorted voices | 0.727 | 0.726 | 0.801 | 0.853 | 0.873 | 6 | 4, 4, 2, 2, 4, 2 |
| recurrent (installed) | 0.736 | 0.726 | 0.784 | 0.843 | 0.867 | 6 | 4, 4, 2, 4, 8, 2 |
| convolutional, all names | 0.764 | 0.755 | 0.808 | 0.844 | 0.861 | 6 | 4, 2, 2, 2, 4, 2 |
| recurrent, pair loss | 0.768 | 0.759 | 0.808 | 0.850 | 0.866 | 6 | 2, 2, 2, 4, 2, 2 |

Rank correlation between telling apart and following the convention: 1.000 at one example a syllable, 0.976 at two, 0.857 at four, 0.786 at eight; linear correlation 0.996, 0.985, 0.975, 0.967; one point of telling apart is worth 1.05 to 1.17 points of convention followed. All three predictions HELD: the rank correlation at four examples is 0.857; the label-free listener follows conventions half a point above the bands at four examples; every listener at 0.69 or better passes four or six sets where the bands pass none.

This is the flagship answered as a dose and a response, in a language none of the eight heard: the better a way of hearing tells two phones apart across speakers, the fewer grounded examples it needs to learn which difference the language uses, in rank order at the smallest dose and nearly so at every dose. From the bands to the best listener, 16 points of telling apart turn "never within eight examples" into "two examples a syllable in five sets of six". The ordering flattens at eight examples because the four best listeners all sit at 0.86 to 0.87 there.

### Several examples of each kind from the reference speaker. Statement (01:07)

This is not the frozen target, which is one example of each kind, and is not reported as it. It tests the reading of 00:36, that what keeps even the same voice under 0.90 is the single example. Same stage-two test, but the reference speaker gives three or five tokens of A and as many of B (all with the same following phone), and X goes with the kind whose tokens are nearer on average. LibriSpeech and Italian; Spanish speakers have half a minute of speech each and cannot give five. Pairs count as before, among cells where the speaker has enough tokens.

Predictions: with five examples the installed listener's mean of four families rises from 0.789 to at least 0.86 on LibriSpeech (chance 0.5) and from 0.728 to at least 0.80 in Italian (0.5); the four listeners together with five examples reach 0.90 in at least two families on LibriSpeech (0.4) and not in all four (0.6).

Outcome (01:22), for the statement of 01:07. Stage two, X from another speaker, the reference speaker giving one, three or five tokens of each kind; voicing, place, manner, vowel and their mean:

| Test | listener(s) | examples of each kind | voicing | place | manner | vowel | mean |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
