What it shows: one frame of these rows already says what the whole token says, so the errors are not at the edges of tokens or in how frames are aligned; they are in what a frame is taken to be. English voicing on kept-out Common Voice speakers is the lowest cell of all (0.60).

Statement (13:42). Teach the angle, since the angle is what is compared. The listener's layer is compared by cosine, but it is fitted through an ordinary last layer in which a row's length and each symbol's offset also carry the name. Fit it instead so that only the angle can: the name's score is the cosine between the layer's row and the symbol's direction, times a fixed scale (kind `angular`, scale 16; `angular32`). Twenty seconds on the card, all names, against `ha` (0.776 by the layer's numbers, 0.796 by naming chances). Prediction: the layer's numbers gain 1 to 3 points; naming chances within a point. Kept if the numbers gain a point or more; otherwise closed.

Outcome (13:44), for the statement of 13:42 (teach the angle). Twenty seconds, all names, kept-out speakers:

| Entrant | updates | layer's numbers | naming chances |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| ordinary last layer (`ha`) | 2,499 | 0.776 | 0.796 |
| named by angle, scale 16 | 2,105 | 0.773 | 0.797 |
| named by angle, scale 32 | 2,017 | 0.770 | 0.790 |

FAILED: no gain on the layer's numbers (−0.3 and −0.6). Closed. The layer's angles already say what the names say; how the last layer reads them is not where the points are.

Measurement (13:46). How much of the gap is the change of voice. The joined rows, the same triplets but X from the speaker who gave A and B (`abx.py … within`), voicing / place / manner / vowel and their mean:

| | Spanish | Italian | LibriSpeech dev-clean |
|---|---|---|---|
| X from another speaker (the target's test) | 0.781 0.784 0.769 0.791, 0.781 | 0.731 0.789 0.802 0.786, 0.777 | 0.770 0.861 0.900 0.788, 0.830 |
| X from the same speaker | 0.915 0.913 0.906 0.897, 0.908 | 0.842 0.872 0.878 0.848, 0.860 | 0.877 0.928 0.937 0.875, 0.904 |

Within one voice the rows tell the families apart at 0.84 to 0.94, at or near the bar in Spanish and English; the change of voice costs 7 to 13 points. So the corpus alignment is not what holds the score under 0.90, as supposed at 13:14: the voice is. With the outcome of 13:41 (one frame says what the token says), what is missing is one thing, a frame's row moving with who speaks.

Statement (13:46). Move each voice's sounds onto the common ones, from that voice's own unnamed speech. For every speaker of the test speech, take all of that speaker's frames, let the listener's own naming chances say how much each frame belongs to each symbol, and average the rows by symbol: that voice's own place for each sound. Do the same over all speakers: the common place. Shift every frame by the difference, weighted by its naming chances, and bring it back to unit length (`abx.py … adapt`; a voice's place for a symbol is shrunk towards the common one as if 20 common frames had been heard, so a symbol the voice hardly says is hardly moved). No names of the test speech, no refit; the listener hears more of the voice before it judges, which a listener meeting an unfamiliar voice may do. Kept-out Catalan and English speakers, `conv240` (0.859 and 0.728 unadapted). Prediction: the mean of four families gains 2 to 5 points in both; a gain under a point closes it. If it holds on the choosing speakers it is given one answer on the three tests, and reported as discrimination after hearing more of the voice, beside and not in place of the one-shot figures.

Outcome (13:48), for the statement of 13:46 (move each voice's sounds onto the common ones). Kept-out speakers, `conv240`, voicing / place / manner / vowel and mean:

| | Catalan | English |
|---|---|---|
| unadapted | 0.851 0.881 0.868 0.835, 0.859 | 0.603 0.793 0.788 0.731, 0.728 |
| each voice's symbols moved onto the common ones | 0.823 0.884 0.871 0.840, 0.854 | 0.618 0.796 0.789 0.735, 0.735 |

FAILED: −0.5 and +0.7, where 2 to 5 was predicted. Closed, and not given to the tests. A voice's sounds are not displaced as wholes by symbol in a way the listener's own naming can find: where it misnames a voice it also moves it wrongly (Catalan voicing loses 2.8).

Measurement (13:48). Does a listener name the speakers it was fitted on? Share of frames named as the corpus names them on the original speech, about 400 utterances of fitting speakers and 517 of kept-out speakers (all frames / speech frames only):

| Listener | fitting speakers | kept-out speakers |
|---|---|---|
| `conv240` (five convolutions, 201.5 h, 13 passes, 133 symbols) | 0.740 / 0.608 | 0.627 / 0.476 |
| `deep240` (recurrent, 201.5 h, 5,117 updates, 133 symbols) | 0.775 / 0.664 | 0.677 / 0.549 |
| installed recurrent listener (22.8 h, 6,000 updates, 84 symbols) | 0.883 / 0.829 | 0.758 / 0.669 |

Two things at once. The listeners on 201.5 hours do not fit their own speakers (0.61 and 0.66 of speech frames, where the recurrent one on 22.8 hours fits 0.83 of its own), so they are short of capacity or of time, not of speech. And every listener names kept-out speakers 10 to 16 points worse than its own, the same change of voice that costs the 7 to 13 points of telling apart. Naming and telling apart do not rank the listeners alike (`conv240` names worst and tells apart best by its layer), so headroom in naming is not a promise.

Statement (13:48). Is capacity what binds at a minute? For the five-convolution listener twenty seconds gave the same score at widths 128, 192 and 256 (updates bound), and from one to four minutes the 256-wide one gained 0.2 points by its layer (0.792 to 0.794), so something other than time binds there. One test, one minute, all names, `bank`, seed 1: 256 wide against 512 wide (`broad`). Prediction: if capacity binds, 512 wide gains at least 1.5 points by the layer's numbers on kept-out speakers; I put the chance at about one in three, since width did nothing at twenty seconds and a 512-wide recurrent listener fitted badly. A gain under a point closes width at this scale and nothing wider or longer is run.

Outcome (13:52), for the statement of 13:48 (is capacity what binds at a minute). One minute, all names, kept-out speakers:

| Width | updates | frames named | layer's numbers | naming chances |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 256 | 7,608 | 0.628 | 0.788 | 0.806 |
| 512 | 1,788 | 0.617 | 0.780 | 0.793 |

FAILED: −0.8 by the layer's numbers, where capacity binding would have given +1.5. The wider listener is a quarter as fast and ends lower. Width is closed at this scale; nothing wider or longer is run.

Statement (13:52). Teach that a voice does not matter, on every frame. The diagnosis of 13:46 and 13:48 is one thing: rows move with who speaks. Distorting a cut as another voice (`voices`) shows the listener more voices but never says that two voices of one cut are the same sounds. Say it: hear every cut twice, each time as a different made voice, name both, and add the distance between the two hearings' rows frame by frame, one minus their cosine, weighted `agree` (`race.py … voices agree=1`). It needs no names for that term and halves the updates. Twenty seconds, all names, `bank`, seed 1, against the same fit with `voices` alone and against `ha` (0.776 / 0.796). Prediction: `voices` alone within a point of `ha` (as with all names before); with `agree=1` the layer's numbers gain 1 to 3 points over `voices` alone on kept-out speakers. I put it at even odds: the made voices are a crude picture of real ones. A gain under a point closes it.

Outcome (13:55), for the statement of 13:52 (teach that a voice does not matter). Twenty seconds, all names, kept-out speakers:

| Entrant | updates | layer's numbers | naming chances |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| as recorded (`ha`) | 2,499 | 0.776 | 0.796 |
| every cut as a made voice | 2,410 | 0.774 | 0.778 |
| every cut as two made voices that must agree | 1,088 | 0.762 | 0.773 |

`voices` alone within a point of `ha` by the layer's numbers: HELD (−0.2). The agreement term: FAILED, −1.2 against `voices` alone at less than half the updates. Closed. Rows that agree between two made voices are not rows that agree between two people.

Statement (13:55). Build the change of voice into the listener's first look. A longer or shorter vocal tract moves a sound's peaks along the frequency bands, which are spaced nearly evenly in the logarithm of frequency above 1 kHz: to a first approximation another voice is the same picture shifted along the bands. Every listener here treats the 40 bands as 40 unrelated inputs to a convolution over time, so nothing it learns at one place along the bands is known at the next; `voices` tries to teach the shift by examples. A convolution over time and bands together, with the largest of neighbouring bands taken twice (40 bands to 10), has the shift built in: the same detectors at every band, and a peak that moves by a band or two leaves the result unchanged. Kind `plane`: two such layers of 32 detectors, then the five convolutions over time as before. Twenty seconds, all names, `bank`, seed 1, against `ha` (0.776 / 0.796 from 2,499 updates). Prediction: fewer updates (1,200 to 2,000), and the layer's numbers gain 1 to 3 points on kept-out speakers; even odds. If it gains a point or more it gets a second seed and a minute; under a point closes it.

Outcome (13:58), for the statement of 13:55 (the change of voice built into the first look). Twenty seconds, all names, kept-out speakers:

| Entrant | updates | frames named | layer's numbers | naming chances |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| bands as unrelated inputs (`ha`) | 2,499 | 0.606 | 0.776 | 0.796 |
| time and bands together (`plane`) | 740 | 0.604 | 0.779 | 0.789 |

By the criterion stated: FAILED, +0.3 by the layer's numbers where a point was needed, and far fewer updates than predicted (740, not 1,200 to 2,000). It is not scaled. One thing in it is not a failure: it reaches `ha`'s score and `ha`'s naming with 30% of `ha`'s updates, and listeners of the first kind stood at 0.734 to 0.762 with 425 to 1,567 updates. An update teaches it about three times as much; the two layers over the whole 300 by 40 picture cost it the time.

Statement (13:58). The same first look made cheap, one change. Halve the bands inside each of the two convolutions (a stride of two along the bands) in place of taking the largest afterwards, 16 detectors in the first and 32 in the second, then the largest of neighbours once (40 bands to 5): about a tenth of the arithmetic (kind `plane16`). Twenty seconds, all names, seed 1. Prediction: at least 1,800 updates, and the layer's numbers at 0.79 or above on kept-out speakers (1.5 points over `ha`). Under 0.786 closes the idea for good; at or above it, a second seed and one minute.

Outcome (14:00), for the statement of 13:58 (the same first look made cheap). Twenty seconds, all names, seed 1, kept-out speakers:

| Entrant | updates | frames named | layer's numbers | naming chances |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| bands as unrelated inputs (`ha`) | 2,499 | 0.606 | 0.776 | 0.796 |
| time and bands together (`plane`) | 740 | 0.604 | 0.779 | 0.789 |
| the same made cheap (`plane16`) | 1,605 | 0.621 | 0.792 | 0.808 |
| for scale: `conv240`, four minutes | 20,261 | 0.638 | 0.794 | 0.808 |

Updates at least 1,800: missed (1,605). The layer's numbers at 0.79 or above: HELD (0.792, +1.6 over `ha`; naming chances +1.2, frames named +1.5). In twenty seconds it stands where the five-convolution listener stood after four minutes. By the branch stated it gets a second seed at twenty seconds and one fit of a minute (running: `q2`, `q60`). Predictions for those, before they return: seed 2 within a point of seed 1 (0.782 to 0.802); the minute at 0.800 or above by the layer's numbers, against 0.788 for the plain listener's minute (`w256`). If the minute is under 0.795 the gain is one of pace alone and levels off where the others did.

Outcome (14:03), for the follow-up fits stated at 14:00. All names, kept-out speakers:

| Entrant | time | updates | frames named | layer's numbers | naming chances |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| plain, seed 1 (`ha`) | 20 s | 2,499 | 0.606 | 0.776 | 0.796 |
| `plane16`, seed 1 | 20 s | 1,605 | 0.621 | 0.792 | 0.808 |
| `plane16`, seed 2 | 20 s | 1,598 | 0.618 | 0.782 | 0.799 |
| plain (`w256`) | 60 s | 7,608 | 0.628 | 0.788 | 0.806 |
| `plane16` | 60 s | 4,832 | 0.648 | 0.797 | 0.816 |
| plain (`conv240`) | 240 s | 20,261 | 0.638 | 0.794 | 0.808 |

Seed 2 within a point of seed 1: HELD at the edge (0.782). The minute at 0.800 or above: FAILED (0.797); it is above 0.795, so not pace alone by the letter, but the honest reading is close to it: a point over the plain listener at equal time at twenty seconds (two seeds: +1.1 and +0.8 by the two readouts) and at a minute (+0.9 and +1.0), and its minute equals the plain listener's four minutes with more frames named (0.648 against 0.638). Seeing a voice's shift along the bands with the same detectors makes the listener learn about four times as fast and names more frames; it does not lift where the score levels off by more than a point. Standing: `plane16` is the better way to make the second listener (a quarter of the time for the same result) and is kept in `listener.py` for the next fit; it is not a route to 0.90, and it does not replace the shipped listener for 0.3 points.

Where the first target stands after today's fourteen short tests. Raised by: the second listener and joining it with the first (+4 to +5 points on the three tests). Not raised by: whitening, a linear map, pooling a token, centring, stretching the bands by the listener's sureness, several listeners judged together, a wider view, the middle of a token, naming by angle, what a symbol says of its sound, two made voices that must agree, moving a voice's sounds by the listener's own naming, more width, more time. Located: within one voice the rows score 0.86 to 0.91; the change of voice costs 7 to 13 points; every listener names its own speakers 10 to 16 points better than kept-out ones and the larger ones do not even fit their own. The threshold of 0.90 across voices in every family is unreached on natural speech and is reported so.

Measurement (14:10). The three things kept apart, the equal-budget comparison, and the installed package checked again, all run in this session.

Discrimination, phonology and IPA literacy for five ways of hearing unheard Spanish (cross-speaker ABX, mean of four families; share of probes read as the taught convention requires at one and at eight examples a syllable, with the sets at 0.80 under both conventions; share of speech frames named as the corpus writes them):

| Way of hearing | names it was fitted with | hearing a difference | learning its use, 1 and 8 examples (sets passing) | naming in IPA |
|---|---|---:|---|---:|
| frequency bands | none | 0.604 | 0.627, 0.752 (0 of 6) | not applicable |
| label-free listener, with a reading layer taught 2.0 h of names on its frozen rows | none in the rows | 0.608 | 0.650, 0.761 (1 of 6) | 0.279 |
| the same network taught those 2.0 h of names end to end | 2.0 h | 0.700 | 0.742, 0.855 (4 of 6) | 0.364 |
| installed recurrent listener | 22.8 h | 0.736 | 0.783, 0.897 (6 of 6) | 0.440 |
| both listeners (the package since 0.7) | 22.8 h and 201.5 h | 0.781 | 0.824, 0.916 (6 of 6) | 0.486 (the second listener's names) |

The equal-budget pair is rows two and three, rerun today: Spanish 0.279 against 0.364, Italian 0.272 (broad 0.287) against 0.342 (broad 0.368). With the same two hours of names, teaching the whole network names 8 to 9 points more frames than a reading layer on rows learned without names, and those rows hear a difference no better than the bands: discovery as built here adds nothing, and what the package ships was taught. The three measures rise together down the table, which is the flagship's answer: each point of hearing a difference is about a point of learning its use.

The installed package, final wheel (`nca-phonetics` 0.9.0 with `nca-grid-navigation` 0.1.0 in the clean consumer environment), two fresh processes a language, every clip heard by a request to the kernel:

| | Welsh third | Ukrainian third |
|---|---|---|
| digest of all frames heard, process 1 and 2 | 46373741e9b4aee1 twice | d69234b0d505b9fa twice |
| examples needed: control / listener / control with listener | 26.0 / 21.0 / 15.5 in both | 13.5 / 10.0 / 9.0 in both |
| also hearing the rest | 18.5 / 20.0 / 12.0 in both | 10.0 / 10.0 / 8.0 in both |
| a clip through the kernel, median and 95th percentile | 5.3 and 8.1 ms | 5.0 and 7.5 ms |
| speech heard, peak memory | 184.5 s, 671 to 675 MB | 172.6 s, 674 to 675 MB |

Five tokens of each kind from the reference speaker in place of one (`abx.py … shots=5`, the joined rows; not the frozen test, which gives one): Spanish 0.847 0.900 0.868 0.862, Italian 0.825 0.899 0.881 0.863, LibriSpeech 0.874 0.943 0.950 0.829. Place reaches the bar in all three and manner in English; voicing and vowel quality do not, even so. The film is 85.7 seconds in seven scenes (title, hearing a difference in another voice, the same sounds in two languages, knowing what was not taught, finding it in running speech, a failure: a long consonant, what the foundation buys).

### Is the cost of a new voice the cost of an unfamiliar voice? Statement (14:20)

Three things have each been multiplied on the same test and left it near 0.80 on kept-out speakers: the speech (ten times), the listener (two and four times as wide), the time (four times). Before more speech from more voices is asked for, the one thing it could buy has to be shown to exist. More voices can only help if a voice the listener was fitted on is told apart better than one it was not. Every listener names its own speakers 10 to 16 points better (13:48), but naming and telling apart have not ranked alike today.

Test, no refit. The stage-two test exactly as on kept-out speakers (A and B from one speaker, X from another, same following phone, one token of each), but among speakers the listener was fitted on: Catalan and English speakers of the original speech with `share % 20 == 1`, a group the same size as the kept-out one (`share % 20 == 0`), by the rule fixed here before any score (`abx.py <language> <listener> fit`). Listeners: `conv240` by its layer (kept-out: Catalan 0.859, English 0.729, mean 0.794) and the shipped joined rows (0.868 and 0.747). This is an upper bound on what familiar voices give, since these speakers' utterances were themselves named in the fit.

Predictions: among fitted speakers `conv240` scores at least 3 points higher on the mean of the two languages (0.5); the joined rows at least 2 points higher (0.5). Branches fixed now: under 1.5 points for both, unfamiliar voices are not what binds, more voices is closed as a route and no download is asked for; 3 points or more, one 20-second refit with a tenth of the fitted speakers' utterances held out separates a familiar voice from a remembered utterance.

Outcome (14:24), for the statement of 14:20. First fitted group (`share % 20 == 1`) against the kept-out group, mean of four families and of Catalan and English: `conv240` 0.813 against 0.794 (+1.9; at least 3 predicted: FAILED), the joined rows 0.850 against 0.808 (+4.2; at least 2 predicted: HELD). Neither branch as written: one listener under 3, the other over. The two languages disagreed (Catalan −2.0 and +0.5, English +5.8 and +7.9) on groups of 29 to 46 speakers, so, decided after seeing this, four more fitted groups (`fit=2` to `fit=5`) were scored to measure the spread between groups, and the recurrent listener alone:

| Listener, readout | kept-out group | five fitted groups, mean (spread of a group, Catalan and English) | difference |
|---|---:|---|---:|
| `conv240`, its layer | 0.794 | 0.800 (0.013, 0.017) | +0.6 |
| installed recurrent listener, its 64 numbers | 0.783 | 0.839 (0.015, 0.013) | +5.6 |
| the same, its naming chances | 0.805 | 0.886 (0.015, 0.008) | +8.1 |
| joined rows | 0.808 | 0.836 (0.011, 0.016) | +2.9 |

By family, the recurrent listener's naming chances among fitted speakers: Catalan 0.891 0.928 0.938 0.900 (voicing, place, manner, vowel), English 0.783 0.918 0.881 0.847.

Reading. The convolutional listener, which names 0.61 of its own speakers' speech frames, tells them apart no better than strangers: a voice it has heard is no help to it. The recurrent one, which names 0.83 of its own speakers' speech frames and 0.67 of a stranger's, tells its own apart 6 to 8 points better, and these were utterances it was fitted on, so a remembered utterance and a familiar voice are not separated here; the refit set as the branch (a tenth of each fitted speaker's utterances held out) would leave one utterance a speaker, too few for a triplet, and was not run. Two things follow that were not known this morning. First, the test does not cap below the bar: where frames are named well, one token of each kind across two voices is told apart at 0.89 to 0.94 in Catalan in all four families (English voicing stays at 0.78 even there, so that cell is partly the corpus's own writing of voicing). Second, 16 points of naming are worth 8 of telling apart for one listener on one test, so the distance to the bar is a distance in naming a stranger's frames: about 0.83 of speech frames where 0.48 to 0.67 are named now. Unfamiliar voices bind only for a listener that fits its speech; none on the 201.5 hours does yet.

One more gap is visible in the same table and has not been measured as such: kept-out Catalan speakers score 0.868 by the joined rows, Spanish and Italian 0.781 and 0.777. Catalan was fitted on; Spanish and Italian never. That difference is as large as the cost of the voice.

### What a language never fitted costs, and what more languages buy. Statement (14:26)

Take Catalan out of the fit and it becomes what Spanish and Italian are: a language the listener never heard. Eight 20-second fits of the quick listener (`plane16`, `bank`, seed 1, as `q1`: Catalan kept-out speakers 0.854 by its layer and 0.865 by its naming chances with all eight languages), each on a subset of the fitted languages named by a new option `only=`; equal time, so equal updates, not equal hours. Scored on the kept-out Catalan speakers as before, and on the kept-out English ones, who are also strangers in language for the arms without English.

| Arm | fitted languages |
|---|---|
| `g1a`, `g1b` | French; Belarusian |
| `g2a`, `g2b` | French, English; Belarusian, Kinyarwanda |
| `g4a`, `g4b` | French, English, German, Belarusian; Belarusian, Kinyarwanda, Bashkir, Swahili |
| `g7` | all but Catalan |
| `g7e` | all but English |

Predictions: without Catalan (`g7`) the Catalan speakers fall at least 4 points by the layer, to 0.814 or under (0.6); without English (`g7e`) the English speakers fall at least 4 points from 0.729 (0.5); from one language to seven the Catalan score rises at least 2 points a doubling of languages on both orders (0.5), and the order with French ahead of the order without at one, two and four languages (0.7). Branches: if `g7` is within 2 points of `q1`, language is not the gap and the difference to Spanish is the corpus; if the rise per doubling is 2 points or more and not flattening from four to seven, more languages is a route with a measured rate and the ask to the user is sized by it.

Outcome (14:33), for the statement of 14:26. Eight 20-second fits of `plane16` on the L40 (1,586 to 1,597 updates each), kept-out speakers, by the layer and by the naming chances:

| Arm | fitted languages | hours | speakers | Catalan | English |
|---|---|---:|---:|---|---|
| `g1a` | French | 27.4 | 377 | 0.781, 0.777 | 0.702, 0.702 |
| `g1b` | Belarusian | 26.1 | 204 | 0.799, 0.780 | 0.685, 0.666 |
| `g2a` | French, English | 60.8 | 1,805 | 0.815, 0.809 | 0.745, 0.791 (fitted) |
| `g2b` | Belarusian, Kinyarwanda | 56.4 | 225 | 0.795, 0.781 | 0.701, 0.673 |
| `g4a` | French, English, German, Belarusian | 108.1 | 2,193 | 0.820, 0.808 | 0.709, 0.747 (fitted) |
| `g4b` | Belarusian, Kinyarwanda, Bashkir, Swahili | 85.1 | 310 | 0.807, 0.808 | 0.694, 0.667 |
| `g7` | all but Catalan | 167.0 | 2,299 | 0.819, 0.809 | 0.713, 0.756 (fitted) |
| `g7e` | all but English | 168.0 | 1,952 | 0.853, 0.863 (fitted) | 0.706, 0.701 |
| `q1` | all eight | 201.5 | 3,380 | 0.854, 0.865 (fitted) | 0.729, 0.752 (fitted) |

Without Catalan the Catalan speakers fall at least 4 points by the layer: FAILED by half a point (0.819, −3.5; −5.6 by the naming chances). Without English the English speakers fall at least 4: FAILED by the layer (−2.3; −5.1 by the naming chances). At least 2 points a doubling of languages on both orders: FAILED (French first: +3.4, +0.5, −0.1; Belarusian first: −0.4, +1.2, +1.2; by the naming chances both orders stand at 0.808 to 0.809 from four languages on). French first ahead at one, two and four: FAILED at one (Belarusian alone 0.799, French alone 0.781), held at two and four.

Reading, by the branches set before. A language never fitted costs 3.5 to 5.6 points on the same speakers, so about half of what separates Spanish and Italian (0.78) from kept-out Catalan (0.87) is that they were never fitted, which is what the goal asks of them. More languages do not buy it back at this size of fit: from four languages to seven the stranger's score does not move (0.820 to 0.819, and 0.808 to 0.809), and it does not matter whether the four bring 2,193 voices or 310. More languages is closed as a route by the rule fixed before the run, and with it the last reason found today to ask for more named speech: ten times the hours, seven times the voices and twice the languages each leave a 20-second-to-4-minute listener within a point. No download is asked for on this evidence. What the fitted-speaker measurement points at instead is a listener that names a stranger's frames far better (0.83 of speech frames where 0.48 to 0.67 are named), and nothing measured here says a few minutes on this speech can produce one.

### The quick first look in front of the recurrent listener. Statement (14:35)

The one listener whose naming chances lead on kept-out speakers is the recurrent one (`deep240`: 0.817, the convolutional ones 0.808 to 0.816), and it is the slow learner: 425 updates in 20 seconds give 0.734 by its 64 numbers and 0.743 by its naming chances (`all20`), 1,282 in a minute 0.757 and 0.794, 5,117 in four minutes 0.779 and 0.817, still rising 2.3 points for four times the updates. The first look over time and bands together made the convolutional listener learn about four times as fast. One change: the same cheap first look (16 then 32 detectors, bands 40 to 5) in place of the bands as the recurrent listener's input (`deepplane`), 20 seconds, no other option, seeds 0 and 1; `all20` is run again with seed 1 for the spread of the mark.

Predictions: at least 380 updates in the 20 seconds (0.6); both seeds at least 1.5 points over the mean of the two `all20` fits on both readouts (0.35). Branches: under 1.5 points, closed and not scaled; 1.5 or more on both seeds, one minute against `all60`, and only if that holds by 1.5 again is a four-minute fit run against `deep240`.

Outcome (14:37), for the statement of 14:35. Kept-out speakers, mean of Catalan and English, by the 64 numbers and by the naming chances: `deepplane` 0.754 and 0.774 (seed 0, 433 updates), 0.755 and 0.766 (seed 1, 437); `all20` 0.734 and 0.743 (seed 0, 425 updates), 0.749 and 0.763 (seed 1, 524 updates, having run last on a freer machine). At least 380 updates: HELD. Both seeds at least 1.5 points over the mean of the two marks (0.742 and 0.753) on both readouts: FAILED (+1.3 and +1.4 by the numbers, +2.1 and +1.3 by the naming chances). The two marks differ by as much as the gain, and by their updates. About a point, as for the convolutional listener; closed by the branch set before and not scaled.

### Overhearing a language nobody named. Statement (14:38)

A language never fitted costs 3.5 to 5.6 points and more named languages do not buy it back (14:33). A learner set down in a new language hears a great deal of it before anyone explains a word. Overhearing unexplained clips cut the examples a word needs by a third this morning; the same idea one level down: the listener fitted without Catalan (`g7`) names unnamed Catalan speech itself, and a new listener is fitted for the same 20 seconds with half of every batch drawn from that speech under those names (`race.py … only=<the seven> teacher=g7`; with `only` and a teacher the unnamed speech is now the fitting speakers of the languages left out). The Catalan heard is that of fitting speakers only; the kept-out Catalan speakers, who are scored, are never heard. No Catalan name, boundary or transcript enters. Two arms: the teacher's likeliest name for each frame (`o1`), and its chances for every symbol (`o1s`, `soft`). Mark: `g7`, Catalan kept-out speakers 0.819 by the layer and 0.809 by the naming chances; with Catalan named (`q1`) 0.854 and 0.865.

Predictions: at least one arm at least 1.5 points over `g7` on Catalan by both readouts (0.35), though it sees half as many named cuts in its 20 seconds; neither arm more than a point under `g7` on Catalan (0.7). Branches: under 1.5 points, closed; 1.5 or more, a second round (the new listener as teacher) on Catalan, and only if that still gains is the same done once, and reported apart from the frozen test, with the unnamed Spanish and Italian speech on disk.

Outcome (14:41), for the statement of 14:38. Catalan kept-out speakers, by the layer and by the naming chances: `g7` (no Catalan) 0.819 and 0.809; `o1` (overhearing 34.5 h of unnamed Catalan under the teacher's likeliest names, 1,411 updates) 0.829 and 0.819; `o1s` (under its chances for every symbol, 1,397 updates) 0.832 and 0.828; with Catalan named 0.854 and 0.865. English, named in all three, is unchanged (0.713 and 0.756; 0.724 and 0.756; 0.718 and 0.747). At least one arm 1.5 points over `g7` by both readouts: FAILED (+1.3 by the layer, +1.9 by the naming chances for the better arm). Neither arm more than a point under: HELD.

Reading. Hearing a language nobody named, under the listener's own naming, buys back about a third of what never having it named costs (1.3 of 3.5 points, 1.9 of 5.6), in the same 20 seconds and with half as many named cuts, and the soft names do a little better than the hard ones. It is the first change today that moves the stranger's score in the right direction by more than a point on one readout, and it is under the bar set before the run on the other, at the size of the difference between two seeds. Closed by the rule; not taken to Spanish and Italian, whose scores therefore stay those of a language never heard at all.

Where the first target stands at 14:41. Four more short tests this afternoon. Found: the test allows the bar (a listener that names 0.83 of its speakers' speech frames tells their sounds apart at 0.89 to 0.94 across two voices in Catalan, one token each); what is missing is naming a stranger's frames, and half of the distance for Spanish and Italian is that they were never fitted. Not routes at this size of fit: more voices (a voice already heard is no help to a listener that does not fit its speech), more languages (flat from four to seven), the quick first look before the recurrent listener (+1), overhearing the unnamed language (+1.3 to +1.9). The threshold of 0.90 across voices in every family stays unreached on natural speech.

### Four listeners side by side, by what they name. Statement (14:44)

Two things each raised the stranger's score and have not been put together: listeners of different make side by side (the recurrent with the convolutional, +1.4 over the better of the two on kept-out speakers, +4 to +5 on the three tests over the first alone), and the root of the naming chances in place of the layer (+1.4 to +3.8 for a single listener). The fitted-speaker measurement of 14:24 says the naming chances are where a listener's knowledge of a sound sits. Since this morning there are four listeners of three makes on disk: the installed recurrent one (22.8 h), `deep240` (recurrent, 201.5 h), `conv240` (convolutional) and `q60` (the first look over time and bands). No refit. Three arms on kept-out Catalan and English speakers, mean of four families and of the two languages (the shipped pair by its layers: 0.808): the shipped pair by naming chances (`deep+post&conv240+post`), the four by their layers, the four by naming chances.

Predictions: the four by naming chances at least 0.835 (0.5); the pair by naming chances at least a point over the pair by layers (0.6); the four by layers under the four by naming chances (0.7). Branches: the four by naming chances under 0.828 (2 points over the shipped pair), closed; at or over it, Spanish, Italian and LibriSpeech are scored once with that arm, and it is a candidate for the package only if all three means rise by 2 points.

Outcome (14:45), kept-out speakers only, nothing of the three tests seen yet. All three predictions HELD (the first by half a thousandth). By the branch the four by naming chances are scored once on Spanish, Italian and LibriSpeech. Added before any of those scores is seen: the pair by naming chances is scored there too, as a second arm, because it would cost the package no new listener; the same rule of 2 points on all three means applies to each arm.

### Do the four listeners' rows teach words from fewer examples? Statement (14:56)

The candidate costs the package two more listeners (one recurrent), about twice the time a clip. This morning 4.5 points of telling apart (the pair against the installed listener alone) did not change the examples eight words need on the choosing vocabularies (52.0, 19.0, 25.0 against 50.0, 22.0, 26.0), though each point was a point of convention followed. Gate before any packaging, on the choosing vocabularies only (Welsh second, Ukrainian first and second; sixteen orders each): `words.py bank <deployment> <arm>` and `learn` with the four listeners by naming chances as the one view.

Predictions: within 3 examples of the pair on each vocabulary (0.6); fewer by a tenth or more on at least two of three (0.25). Branch: packaged only if fewer by a tenth on two of three; otherwise the gain stays one of telling apart, is reported, and the package keeps its two listeners.

Within 3 examples of the pair on each vocabulary: FAILED (Welsh 15.5 more, the second Ukrainian vocabulary 4.5 fewer). Fewer by a tenth on two of three: FAILED (one of three). By the branch the package keeps its two listeners read by their layers. The naming chances tell two phones apart across voices 2.5 points better and teach words no better, and alone clearly worse: a row of naming chances keeps what the listener can name and drops the rest of the sound, which a word in a new language also uses. The gain is one of telling apart and is reported as that: Spanish 0.807, Italian 0.801, LibriSpeech 0.854 by four listeners' naming chances, the best measured on the frozen test, one cell of twelve at the bar.

Measurement (15:03). The three targets set out by language and contrast for the closing report, from the stored result files of the installed rows; nothing refitted. New here only the spread of the teaching saving over the sixteen orders: Welsh 26.0 against 15.5 examples, paired difference 10.5 (95% over resampled orders 7.5 to 14.5; fewer in 14 orders, equal in 2, more in none); Ukrainian 13.5 against 9.0, paired difference 4.5 (2.5 to 7.0; fewer in 8, equal in 8, more in none). Every one of the twenty histories of every convention set is at 0.80 or above at eight examples a syllable (lowest 0.805, Spanish voicing with place).

## Telling sounds apart as one system (goal of 16:10)

### What is frozen before anything is fitted or scored (16:16)

Three regimes, reported apart. (1) The frozen one-token test: Spanish, Italian, LibriSpeech dev-clean, the triplets of `abx.py` unchanged. (2) New voices in taught languages: the speakers with `share("speaker:" + language + speaker) % 20 == 19` in both feature folders (Catalan 64 speakers, English 60, French 23, Belarusian 10, German 8), reserved from this minute: kept out of every fit made from now on and never used to choose anything; a candidate is scored on them once. The 0.9.0 listeners were fitted on these speakers, so regime (2) exists only for listeners refitted without them; Catalan and English carry the bar, the others are reported where a family has enough triplets. (3) Elisa as a system: one request to the kernel may replay, ask a speaker for further tokens, overhear unexplained speech and let several experts answer together; every further token asked of a speaker is charged as one unit of teacher effort and its seconds of sound as time, from the first exposure. The choosing group stays `% 20 == 0`, now from both feature folders (Catalan 56 speakers, English 67). Bar: 0.90 in every one of voicing, place, manner and vowel quality in (2) and in (3) at a stated cost. An idea earns four times its size only with 2 points over two seeds and half its prediction; three ideas in a row under 2 points end the goal.

### Idea 1, no fit: further tokens from both speakers. Statement (16:16)

Theory. Within one voice the rows score 0.86 to 0.91 and five reference tokens were worth 6 to 8 points with X still a single token: the token, not the voice, carries half the error, and X's own token is the half no reference can average away. A system that may ask is not bound to one token: asking X's speaker to say it again averages that half too. If a token's row is its phone's place plus independent scatter, k tokens on each side cut the scatter's variance by k on both sides of the comparison.

Test on the choosing group, both folders, shipped rows (`deep&conv240`), Catalan and English: the same cells for every arm (speakers with at least five tokens of the phone before that following phone), tokens of A and B from the first speaker and of X from the second: 1 and 1, 5 and 1, 1 and 5, 3 and 3, 5 and 5 (`abx.py <language> <arm> dev wide least=5 shots=K xshots=M`). X goes with the kind whose tokens are nearer on average over all pairs of tokens.

Predictions: 5 and 5 at least 10 points over 1 and 1 on the mean of four families in both languages (0.5); 5 and 5 at 0.90 or above in every family in Catalan (0.5) and in at least two families in English (0.5); 1 and 5 worth at least 3 points over 1 and 1 (0.6), X's tokens being the half never averaged before; 3 and 3 above 5 and 1 (0.5), at four further tokens against eight.

Outcome (16:22), for the statement of 16:16. Choosing group from both folders, shipped rows, cells where each speaker has five tokens, 600 triplets a direction; voicing, place, manner, vowel and their mean; further tokens asked beyond the three of the plain test:

| Tokens of A and B / of X | further tokens | Catalan | English |
|---|---:|---|---|
| 1 / 1 | 0 | 0.875 0.865 0.902 0.842, 0.871 | 0.694 0.843 0.879 0.753, 0.792 |
| 1 / 5 | 4 | 0.913 0.911 0.949 0.889, 0.915 | 0.754 0.916 0.916 0.784, 0.843 |
| 5 / 1 | 8 | 0.922 0.913 0.954 0.896, 0.921 | 0.796 0.923 0.928 0.808, 0.864 |
| 3 / 3 | 6 | 0.950 0.935 0.980 0.916, 0.945 | 0.841 0.962 0.957 0.856, 0.904 |
| 5 / 5 | 12 | 0.961 0.945 0.989 0.932, 0.957 | 0.880 0.975 0.977 0.879, 0.928 |

Five and five at least 10 points over one and one in both languages: FAILED in Catalan (+8.6, the score running into its ceiling), held in English (+13.6). Five and five at 0.90 in every Catalan family: HELD. In at least two English families: HELD (place and manner; voicing 0.880, vowel 0.879). X's own tokens worth at least 3 points: HELD (+4.4 and +5.0 for four tokens, more a token than the references give: +5.0 and +7.2 for eight). Three and three above five and one: HELD (0.945 and 0.904 against 0.921 and 0.864), at six further tokens against eight; the statement's count of four was wrong.

Reading. The token is the larger half of the error and a system that may ask removes it: three tokens a side put every Catalan family over the bar and two of four English ones, with no fit and no new listener. Asking X's speaker is the cheapest question, which the earlier five-token test never asked. The idea has earned its next size, which here is not time but use: the three test corpora, the reserved speakers, and a registered operation. Per further token: +1.2 and +1.9 points at three a side, +0.7 and +1.1 at five a side.

### Idea 2, no fit: ask only when unsure. Statement (16:24)

Theory. Most trials are easy: the lead of the nearer kind, (far − near) / (far + near), is large and a further token cannot change the answer. The teacher's effort should go where the lead is small. Start from one token of each kind; while the lead is under a bar, ask for the next token, X's speaker first (the cheaper question by idea 1), then one more of A and of B, in turn, up to three of each. Every token asked is one unit of effort and its length in sound.

Test (`ask.py collect <language> dev wide`, then `ask.py policy`): the choosing group, the installed package's rows from the stored frames, 300 trials a direction for every pair with three cells, five tokens of each kind drawn once so every policy sees the same tokens; bars 0.01 to 0.2. Fixed three and three costs six further tokens.

Predictions: some bar gives a mean within 1 point of fixed three and three for at most 3.5 further tokens a trial in both languages (0.5); at that bar under 70% of trials ask at all (0.5); asking X first is at least as good as asking for references first at equal cost (0.6). Branch: if no bar comes within 1 point at 4 tokens or fewer, asking stays fixed at three and three.

Outcome (16:26), for the statement of 16:24 (idea 2). Choosing group, the installed package's rows, 12,000 Catalan and 6,600 English trials, mean of four families; further tokens asked a trial:

| Policy | Catalan | English |
|---|---|---|
| one of each | 0.874 for 0 | 0.767 for 0 |
| fixed two and two | 0.930 for 3 | 0.833 for 3 |
| fixed three and three | 0.952 for 6 | 0.861 for 6 |
| fixed five and five | 0.964 for 12 | 0.887 for 12 |
| asking under a lead of 0.12, up to three | 0.930 for 0.95 | 0.832 for 1.33 |
| asking under 0.2, up to three | 0.945 for 2.02 (43% of trials ask) | 0.851 for 2.60 (52%) |
| asking under 0.3, up to three | 0.950 for 3.47 | 0.858 for 3.98 |
| asking under 0.2, up to five | 0.953 for 3.46 | 0.873 for 4.74 |

Within a point of fixed three and three for at most 3.5 tokens in both languages: HELD (under 0.2: 0.7 and 1.0 points under it for 2.0 and 2.6 tokens). Under 70% of trials asking: HELD. X first at least as good as references first: HELD by a hair (the same score for 0.05 to 0.1 fewer tokens). Asking only when unsure gives the two-and-two score for one token where the fixed way pays three, and the three-and-three score for a third of its price: 3.5 points a token asked in Catalan against 1.3 for the fixed way. Frozen here for everything that follows: ask under a lead of 0.2, X's speaker first, up to three tokens of each kind.

What tokens do not buy, by pair at five and five: Catalan s/z 0.81, o/u 0.75, m/n 0.87; English s/z 0.72, t/d 0.74, a/o 0.81. These stand still however many tokens are heard, so they are not scatter but a steady difference between what the corpus writes and what the rows hold. This script counted a pair with three cells and drew cells evenly, the frozen test counts a pair with 200 distinct triplets and draws the context first; `ask.py` is brought to the frozen test's rule before anything else is scored with it, and the table above is rerun under it.

### The shipped recipe refitted without the reserved speakers. Statement (16:26)

Regimes (2) and (3) on the reserved speakers need listeners that never heard them. Not a new idea: the two shipped listeners' recipes run again on the box with groups 0 and 19 both kept out, each within four minutes: the recurrent listener on the original 22.8 hours (`r19deep`, 240 s, about 5,000 updates where the installed one made 6,000 in an hour on this machine) and the convolutional one on all 201.5 hours (`r19conv`, 240 s, `bank`). Predictions on the choosing group, pair by layers (shipped pair 0.808): the refitted pair within 1 point either way (0.6); `r19conv` alone within 1 point of `conv240`'s 0.794 (0.7); `r19deep` alone within 1.5 points of the installed listener's 0.783 (0.5), having 17% fewer updates. If the refitted pair is more than 1.5 points under the shipped one, the recurrent recipe is not reproduced by four minutes and its fit is the thing to make faster first.

Rerun under the frozen test's pair rule (16:31, choosing group, 12,600 Catalan and 7,800 English trials): one of each 0.873 and 0.783; fixed three and three 0.943 and 0.900 for 6 tokens; asking under a lead of 0.2 up to three 0.939 (0.942 0.930 0.971 0.913) for 1.98 tokens and 0.889 (0.831 0.950 0.950 0.825) for 2.72. Catalan is over the bar in all four families for two tokens; English voicing and vowel are not, at any number of tokens up to five (0.882 and 0.886).

A refinement of idea 2's rule, stated before it is run (16:31). Measured on the same trials: the same lead is worth more with more tokens behind it (a lead of 0.1 to 0.2 is right 0.82 of the time with one token of each, 0.93 with two and two, 0.97 with three and three), so a fixed lead asks too often late. The chance of being right fits 1 / (1 + exp(−k · lead)) with k about 3.5 n + 3, n the tokens of one kind plus those of X (k = 10, 13, 18, 20, 23 measured for n = 2 to 6). Rule: ask while that chance is under a bar. Prediction: some bar gives the lead rule's score (within 0.2 points) for at least 15% fewer tokens in both languages (0.6). If not, the lead rule stays.

Outcome (16:31). Asking while the chance of being right is under 0.9: Catalan 0.939 for 1.58 further tokens (the lead rule: 0.939 for 1.98), English 0.889 for 2.24 (0.889 for 2.72): the same score for 20% and 18% fewer questions. HELD. Frozen for the package and for every regime: ask while 1 / (1 + exp(−(3.5 n + 3) · lead)) is under 0.9, X's speaker first, then one of each kind, up to three tokens of each. Against one token of each this is +6.6 points in Catalan and +10.6 in English for 1.6 and 2.2 questions: 4.2 and 4.7 points a question.

Note (16:34). `r19deep` at 240 s was stopped by the bench's 290-second limit before it wrote its checkpoint (loading and the closing naming pass take the rest); it is queued again at 200 s, about 4,200 updates. `r19conv` is unaffected.

Throughput, found by profiling (16:46). Both refits were cut off by the bench's limit with nothing written, and the cause was not the fits: on the box the speech frames, read in 17 s this morning, now take over 150 s. Measured there: reading every file from disk 0.85 s; a 200 MB numpy copy 1.9 to 2.6 s where 0.1 s is normal; the same copy 0.11 s with `NUMPY_MADVISE_HUGEPAGE=0`, and one shard's frames loaded in 0.08 s against 0.78 s. The host's memory has fragmented since the morning and numpy's request for huge pages stalls on it. The setting is now exported by `bench.sh`; both refits are rerun at 240 s. No idea was tested here; ten times the loading speed is throughput regained, not gained.

### The asking rule as one request to the kernel (16:50)

Built into the package (0.10.0, 26 tests): `phonetics/tell` takes heard stretches of two kinds and of a fresh sound and how many more of each may be asked for, and answers with the kind, its lead and the chance it is right. While that chance is under 0.9 it asks the kernel, from inside the request, for a heard stretch of whose sound it wants next; the kernel plans that from whatever installed expert gives a marked waveform for `phonetics.say-again` and the package's own `phonetics/listen-in`, which hears a marked stretch within its whole utterance. The package names no speaker; the evaluation installs one beside it. Three experts of two origins in one request: the teller, the speaker, the listener.

Measured from sound in the clean consumer process (`evaluation/tell_apart.py`, the first 40 trials of every pair and direction of the choosing group, as far as 225 s reached):

| English | 863 | 0.777 (0.715 0.842 0.838 0.712) | 0.880 (0.842 0.961 0.929 0.788) | 2.11 | 56% | 43 ms | 1.1 ms / 41 and 323 ms | 393 MB |

### Idea 3, no fit: tell by what the listeners name. Statement (17:10)

What stays under the bar at any number of tokens (s/z, t/d, a/o, o/u) does not shrink with more tokens, so it is a steady disagreement between what the corpus writes and what the layers hold, not scatter. The layers were shaped to hold whatever helps naming; the naming chances themselves hold the corpus's own lines between sounds, which is what the test draws its pairs from, and in a taught language those lines were taught. So for telling apart, and only there (word teaching keeps the shipped rows, which the four-listener test of 14:56 showed it needs), the rows are the root of each listener's naming chances, listeners side by side. No fit: `r19deep` and `r19conv` as they stand, on the choosing group. Arms: the pair by layers (0.802 on one token), by chances, and both side by side. Predictions. One token, mean of Catalan and English: by chances at least 2.0 points over by layers (0.55; alone the two gain 2.8 and 1.5); both side by side within 0.5 of by chances (0.6). With tokens, fixed three and three, cells with five tokens: English voicing and vowel together gain at least 2 points (0.6); Catalan under 1 point (0.6). Earns a next size (two more listeners fitted without groups 0 and 19, four minutes each) only with at least 2 points on one token in both languages or at three and three in the mean; dropped otherwise.

Outcome (17:16), idea 3, choosing group, `r19deep` and `r19conv`; voicing, place, manner, vowel and mean.

The same change on the shipped pair gave +1.5 on one token at 14:45, so over the two pairs of listeners it is +2.9 and +1.5.

With asking, a lead of naming-chance rows is less sure than the same lead of layer rows: the slope measured by `ask.py slope` is 2.0 n + 2.4 (6.0, 8.3, 10.8, 12.5, 14.0 for n = 2 to 6) against 3.4 n + 3.0 for layers, which reproduces the frozen 3.5 n + 3. With asking the gain is 0.5 to 1.6 points for a tenth fewer questions. It gained 2.9 on its stated measure and is not counted among the ideas under 2 points; it stays a reading of the listeners already fitted, at no fitting cost, and a candidate for the telling operation only.

### Idea 4, no fit: tell within one voice, through that voice's overheard speech. Statement (17:16)

Measured on the three tests with the shipped pair: with X from the same speaker as A and B, one token each is right 0.908, 0.860 and 0.904 of the time (Spanish, Italian, LibriSpeech) against 0.781, 0.777 and 0.830 across speakers. Seven to thirteen points are lost in crossing between voices, and shifting a voice's sounds by what the listener names did not recover them (13:48). A comparison across voices can instead be used only to rank: among everything the fresh speaker has been overheard to say, the stretches most like the first speaker's A are that speaker's own A, even when each lies nearer to the first speaker's B in absolute terms, as long as the fresh speaker keeps the two apart at all. X is then compared with those stretches within its own voice. Unexplained speech of the voice is what regime (3) may overhear; it costs time and no teacher effort.

Test, no fit (`overhear.py`): the shipped pair by layers, choosing group, the first 20 trials of every pair and direction among cells with five tokens, one token of A, B and X. From up to 40 other utterances of X's speaker (X's own stretch left out), the five stretches most like A and the five most like B, found by sliding the token over the speech at three rates; X is compared with them by the package's warped distance and the nearer kind wins. The same through the first speaker's speech (stretches like X, compared with A and B). Read by corpus names for diagnosis only: how many of the stretches found are of the kind sought. Predictions (direct, one of each: about 0.873 and 0.783): at least 0.6 of the stretches found for A are named as A (0.6); through X's voice alone within 1 point of direct or above (0.5); direct and overheard leads added gain at least 2 points in both languages (0.45) and at least 3 in English voicing (0.4). Mean gain predicted: 3 points. Next size (as registered operations in one kernel request, then with asking on top) only with at least 2 points in the mean and 1 in each language; dropped otherwise.

Through X's voice alone within a point of direct or above: HELD (+0.8, +1.8). Direct and overheard gaining 2 points in both: FAILED (+1.2, +0.8); English voicing +2.5 where 3 was asked: FAILED. The best of six readings is +1.7 and +1.5. Under 2 points: dropped, the first idea under the bound. What it shows: a comparison through ten stretches of which half are of the wrong kind is as good as the direct one, so the within-voice step is as sharp as measured; the search across voices is where the loss sits, and it is the same crossing as before.

Crossing costs most in English a/o (13 points at five tokens), s/z (8) and Catalan o/u (13) and n/ɲ (21): sounds whose place differs between accents. ### Idea 5: name each frame where the listener hears the phone, not where the corpus put it. Statement (17:26)

The corpus's alignment squeezes some phones into one or two frames (25 November: Catalan ɾ 0.96 and d 0.84 of their intervals at 20 ms or less, Italian v 0.95, d 0.91), so most of what those phones sound like is taught under a neighbour's name. Every listener so far was fitted to those names frame by frame, and every test token is cut by them. If a share of the frames carries a wrong name, each update teaches partly against the sound, which is slower learning from the same speech: the thing this goal asks to raise first. The known remedy is to let a fitted listener lay the corpus's own sequence of phones over the frames again, by its naming chances, each phone getting at least three frames (the best monotone path, silence allowed only where the corpus has a gap), and to fit on those names.

Step one, no fit (`realign.py r19deep sample=400`): how many spoken frames change name, and how long the squeezed phones become. Step two only if at least 5% of spoken frames change: 20 s fits on the box, the convolutional listener on all the speech as `all20b` (0.749 by numbers, 0.763 by naming chances on the choosing group), corpus names against the new names, two seeds each. Predictions: at least 10% of spoken frames change name (0.6); the new names gain 1 point in the mean of the two readouts (expected), at least 2 points with probability 0.2. The test tokens stay cut by the corpus's intervals, which works against the new names for the phones moved most. Next size (240 s) only with 2 points in both seeds; dropped otherwise.

Outcome (17:41), idea 5. Step one, 400 fitting utterances, then all 118,346 on the box in 114 s (naming on the card in batches of one length, paths in forked workers; the first version, naming on one processor thread, took 53 ms an utterance and did not finish in 250 s): 27.3% of the 40.7 million spoken frames change name; stretches of one or two frames go from 5.6% to none; Catalan ɾ from 1.5 to 3.6 frames, n from 6.6 to 8.5, d from 6.0 to 6.7. At least 10% changing: HELD.

Step two, 20 s each on the box, the recurrent listener on all 191.3 hours without groups 0 and 19 (the recipe of `all20b`, which is the recurrent kind, not the convolutional one the statement named), choosing group, mean of Catalan and English:

| Names | seed 0: updates, numbers, chances | seed 1: updates, numbers, chances | mean of both readouts |
|---|---|---|---|
| the corpus's | 447, 0.737, 0.763 | 457, 0.745, 0.754 | 0.750 |
| laid again by `r19deep` | 456, 0.730, 0.771 | 504, 0.734, 0.756 | 0.748 |

−0.2 points in the mean (−0.9 by numbers, +0.5 by naming chances), where 1 was expected and 2 were needed. FAILED and dropped: the second idea in a row under 2 points. A quarter of the frames carrying another name leaves 20 seconds of learning where it was, so the corpus's boundaries are not what slows the fit; the listener that laid the names was itself fitted to those boundaries and mostly gives them back with the squeezed phones widened.

### Idea 6, a fit of under a second: a teller that weighs its evidence. Statement (17:41)

The teller adds the two listeners' rows with equal weight and reads one kind of evidence, how unlike the rows are after time is warped away. Warping removes how long a sound lasts, which is itself a cue the speaker gives (a voiced fricative is shorter than its voiceless partner), and the two listeners and their two readings (layer, naming chances) are not equally sure on every contrast: layers and chances side by side with equal weight were worse than chances alone (idea 3). Replace the fixed sum by weights learned from trials: for each trial five leads (each listener by its layer, each by its naming chances, and the lead in length, |log of X's frames over B's| minus |log of X's frames over A's|), one weight each, fitted by logistic regression on half of the choosing group's trials and gated on the other half, both ways round. No listener is refitted. Baseline: the shipped pair by naming chances with equal weights, the best reading so far, on the same halves. Predictions, one token of each, mean of four families and of Catalan and English: +1.0 over the baseline (expected), at least 2 with probability 0.15; the length lead alone above chance in voicing (at least 0.55) in both languages (0.7); voicing gains at least 1.5 (0.5). With three tokens of each and the same weights: under 1 point (0.7). Next size (the weights in the package's teller, then the reserved speakers once) only with 2 points on one token in both halves; dropped otherwise, and then three ideas in a row are under 2 points.

Outcome (17:47), idea 6 (`weigh.py ca en`), choosing group, cells with five tokens. Weights on the leads over their spread, the two halves: recurrent by layer 0.34 and 0.37, convolutional by layer 0.34 and 0.45, recurrent by naming chances 0.69 and 0.73, convolutional by naming chances 0.89 and 0.77, length 0.09 and 0.02. Mean of four families on the half not fitted on, the pair by naming chances with equal weights against the weighed leads:

| | Catalan, the two halves | English, the two halves |
|---|---|---|
| one of each | 0.887 → 0.890, 0.887 → 0.886 | 0.796 → 0.801, 0.805 → 0.812 |
| three of each | 0.950 → 0.949, 0.952 → 0.953 | 0.913 → 0.914, 0.919 → 0.917 |

+0.1 in Catalan and +0.6 in English on one token, +0.35 in the mean where 1.0 was expected and 2 needed: FAILED. Length alone at 0.55 in voicing: Catalan 0.573, English 0.516, FAILED in English. Voicing gaining 1.5: FAILED (+0.7 and +0.8). Three of each under 1 point: HELD (0.0). Dropped. The fit gives the naming chances twice the weight of the layers and length almost none, which is the reading of idea 3 found again by a fit, and nothing beyond it.

Ideas 4, 5 and 6 each gained under 2 points (+1.6 at best, −0.2, +0.35). By the goal's rule the search stops here and what stands is reported: asking (ideas 1 and 2) as the gain shipped, naming chances (idea 3) as a measured reading not built in.

### The naming-chance reading in the package's teller. Statement (18:04)

Having held on the reserved speakers, the reading is built in (`nca-phonetics` 0.11.0): `phonetics/name-in` gives a marked stretch as the root of the two shipped listeners' naming chances (the naming layers added to the two weight files, every array already there unchanged; largest difference from the research listeners 0.0008), and `phonetics/tell` asks for such stretches under the slope 2.0 n + 2.4. The heard rows, and with them the frozen one-stretch test and word teaching, are untouched. The frozen test's three corpora are scored once with this candidate as the system uses it (`ask.py collect <es|it|ls> rows=deep+p&conv240+p`, the frozen rule), then answered from sound through the kernel in a fresh process, alone, for latency and memory. Predictions against by layers (0.882, 0.913, 0.916 with asking): each mean within 1.5 points, none lower by more than 0.5 (0.6); a tenth fewer questions in at least two (0.6); Italian vowel reaches 0.90 (0.4); Spanish stays under 0.90 in at least two families (0.7).

With a fresh working memory for every request it stayed at 301 MB whatever the count, so the growth is the one working memory keeping every waveform it was given, about 2 MB a trial, and none of it is the package. The evaluation now gives each trial its own working memory: peak 323 MB in both alone runs. ### Idea 7, 20 s of fitting: a layer that cannot tell who is speaking. Statement (18:59)

The user restarted the rented card for this after the stop rule had been reached; it is one more tiny test, not a scaling of anything. Regime (2) is bound by the listener. The recurrent listener tells the speakers it was fitted on apart 6 to 8 points better than strangers (14:24), so its compared layer carries the voice, and what was tried against that worked on made-up voices (distorted cuts, two hearings made to agree) or on shifts found by the listener's own naming. Not tried: asking the layer itself to be useless for recognising the real speaker. A second small network reads each cut's mean and spread of the 64 numbers over time and names which of the 3,212 fitting speakers said it; its error is turned round before it reaches the listener (weight W), so the listener is pushed to keep in those numbers only what does not betray the voice, while still naming every frame.

Test: the recurrent listener on all 191.3 hours for 20 s, as `c20s0` and `c20s1` of this afternoon (0.737 and 0.745 by numbers, 0.763 and 0.754 by naming chances on the choosing group), with `unvoice=0.1` and `unvoice=1`, seeds 0 and 1. Read first: whether the speaker-namer learns at all in 20 s (its loss starts at ln 3212 = 8.07); if it ends above 7.5 in every run the test says nothing and is reported as that. Predictions: by numbers +1.0 for the better weight (expected), at least 2 points over both seeds with probability 0.15; by naming chances within 1 point either way (0.6); frames named falls by under 2 points (0.6). Next size (240 s) only with 2 points in both seeds by either readout; dropped otherwise.

## Asking while unsure as one ability of the system (goal of 19:10)

The goal of 16:10 was reported at its stop rule and cleared by the user at 19:04; idea 7 was stated and coded and never run. The new goal: the telling operation of `nca-phonetics` decides whether to answer or ask through the core's thinking frame (§76) with chances calibrated by the core's own tools; its hand-set slope, its bar of 0.9 and its fixed asking order go. Measured as kernel requests in a clean consumer process: (a) no loss against 0.11.0 at equal questions (within 0.5 points, or equal score for a tenth fewer questions) on the reserved speakers, Spanish, Italian and LibriSpeech; (b) stated chance against observed share right within 3 points on speakers not used to calibrate, per language; (c) three stated costs of a question against a wrong answer move questions and share right as §76 predicts, with no code change; (d) the same frame, unchanged, decides for one expert of another package in another domain, named before measuring. Stop rule: three ideas in a row under half their prediction.

### What the core already has, read before building (19:20)

THEORY.md's index and a grep of `src/nca`:

- The frame, `nca.thinking` (§76): concepts give opinions (hypotheses each with a chance of its own, a risk of going on, a risk of putting out); `think` takes the best hypothesis where its chance reaches the threshold and the risk ratio meets its bound; a hypothesis that is an asking of the kernel (`Request`) is served within the step, its answer noted on the scratchpad under the type asked for, and the concepts asked again, at most `rounds` times (§76.6); with no way to ask given, the asking taken is what `think` returns. `through(kernel, workspace)` gives a kernel asking. Thresholds `τ_p`, `τ_r`.
- What going on is worth: 76.3 (the best of the steps left), 76.9 (taught by the chance at the step where the rule next put out), 76.11 (a step of going on charged a share δ: the rule puts out where c ≥ (1 − δ)v and gives up at most nδv), 76.12. All of it theory and Lean; the code that teaches it lives in packages (`nca-choices`, Codex's learner). 76.7 leaves open "a risk of going on that counts what thinking itself costs".
- Calibration: `nca.ladder.Rung.calibrate` (a temperature fitted on held-out labelled inputs), `nca.evidence.grouped.fit_groups` and `Grouped` (one rung for each group of inputs, §37, with Theorem 37.1 that groups carry across mixtures), `Calibrated` (the calibrated reading and its risk), `nca.stats` (Wilson and Clopper-Pearson bounds), `nca.selective` (§16, answering under a risk target), §17 (calibration under shift), §35 (audits).
- The scratchpad of `nca.memory.working.WorkingMemory` (notes with a life in steps).

What is missing, and so is all that is built: (1) nothing in the core says what an asking is worth. A concept that offers an asking has to bring its chance from somewhere; in 0.11.0 the teller does not use the frame at all and asks while 1/(1 + exp(−(2.0 n + 2.4) lead)) < 0.9, the fresh sound first, then one of each kind. (2) `think` can serve an asking only when it is given the kernel; an expert that the kernel is itself running, as a generator, has no such handle and must yield the asking to whoever runs it. Nothing else is missing: hypotheses, askings, the scratchpad and grouped calibration are there.

### Idea 1: an asking is a hypothesis whose chance is what the answer after it is worth, less the charge. Statement (19:21)

Theory (to be §76.13, with Lean). A **place** is what has been asked so far; each asking leads from a place to a later one, and no run of askings comes back. At a place the concept holds its answers with calibrated chances, the best c, and one hypothesis for each asking a still open there. By 76.9 an asking is right where the rule's next putting out after it is right, and by 76.11 it is charged a share δ, so its chance is w_a(p, c) = (1 − δ) E[W(p_a, c′) | p, c, a] with W(p, c) = max(c, max_a w_a(p, c)), W = c where nothing is open. The frame's own rule (take the hypothesis of greatest chance) is then backward induction: no way of asking and answering is paid more, charges counted. E[· | p, c, a] is read from recorded askings: counts of (place, band of c, asking) → (later place, band of c′). Three consequences are the predictions of (c): a dearer question is worth less at every place, so wherever the rule answers at one charge it answers at every dearer one; the share right cannot rise with the charge; and by 76.11 what a dearer charge gives up is at most δ for each question it did not ask. δ is the cost of a question as a share of what a right answer is worth: the request states it, nothing in the code holds it. The slope goes to `fit_groups`: the teller's scorer gives the two kinds weights exp(±K·lead/2), the group of an input is how many stretches of each it holds, and the rung's temperature for a group is what the hand-set slope stood for.

Generic parts, to the core: the table of recorded askings and the worth read from it; one concept that turns a standing (hypotheses, calibrated chances, place, open askings) into an opinion with the askings among the hypotheses; `think` in generator form for an expert the kernel is running. Domain parts, in the package: the scorer, the grouping, how an asking becomes a request, and the fitted temperatures and counts as data, fitted on the choosing group of Catalan and English (group 0).

Tiny test before any core code, no fitting beyond 27 one-number temperatures and the counts (seconds), offline on the stored distances of the choosing group, where 0.11.0's rule is run beside it: (i) on the English choosing group, calibrated and taught on the Catalan one, and the other way round (the choosing group's own stand-in for a strange language); (ii) on half the trials of each, taught on the other half.

Predictions. At 0.11.0's own number of questions the learned rule's share right is within 0.5 points of 0.11.0's in each of the four cells of (i) and (ii), mean change between −0.3 and +0.8 (the hand rule was tuned on these very trials, so a gain is not expected; the test is whether anything is lost by learning it). Calibration of the final answers, as the sum over ten bands of stated chance of |share right − mean stated| weighted by their trials: at most 3 points in (ii); in (i) between 2 and 6 points, over 3 in at least one direction, because English is the harder language (0.80 against 0.89 with one stretch of each) and counts of stretches cannot see that. Stopping by one's own chance flatters it: without correction the final answers' stated chance is above their share right by 1 to 4 points even in (ii). Drop the idea if the loss at equal questions is over 1 point in two cells.

Outcome (19:22), 3 s a cell, 50 bands of chance, one temperature for each of the 27 counts of stretches; share right as in the reports (mean over families of the mean over pairs), questions = further stretches a trial:

| English → Catalan | 12,600 | 1.36, 0.9436 | 0.9458 | +0.22 | 1.15 (15% fewer) | 1.1 (−1.0) |
| Catalan, half → half | 6,300 | 1.32, 0.9437 | 0.9442 | +0.05 | 1.26 (5% fewer) | 1.0 (+0.4) |

### Idea 2, no fit beyond temperatures: the group of an input is also how far apart the sounds are. Statement (19:23)

Looked at first, on the choosing group: what a lead is worth turns on the level of the distances it is a ratio of, (far + near)/2. With one stretch of each the best slope by tercile of level is 4.5, 7.25, 10.0 on Catalan and 3.5, 6.0, 8.75 on English (cuts 0.38 and 0.46 in both), and with three of each 9.75, 15.5, 20.0 and 10.25, 14.75, 28.0. So a lead between distant sounds is worth about twice one between near sounds, alike in both languages, and the single temperature of a count of stretches averages over it. By Theorem 37.1 calibration by groups carries to any mixture of the groups: if the languages differ partly in how their trials spread over the levels, grouping by level carries better across them. The grouping is the domain's (`fit_groups` takes any function of the input); the core is unchanged by it.

Test (seconds): the four cells of idea 1 with the group = (counts of stretches, band of level), three and five bands cut at the fitting trials' quantiles. Predictions: across languages the error with one stretch of each falls from 2.2 and 3.8 to at most 2.5 in both; the final answers' error at δ = 0.02 is at most 2.5 in all four cells; at 0.11.0's questions the share right gains 0.3 points on idea 1 on the mean of the four cells (a sharper chance stops better). Drop the grouping if the mean of the two cross-language errors with one stretch falls by under 0.5 points.

What it does not do is what it was thought up for: with one stretch of each, the English answers taught on Catalan stay overconfident by 2.8 points, so the levels do not carry the difference between the languages; the gain is a sharper chance inside each. ### Built, and the one scoring of what was built. Statement (19:33)

Built. Core (`nca.thinking`, no task named): `Askings` (the record), `Worth` (what each asking is worth under a charge, from the last places backwards), `Unsure` (the concept: an inquiry's answers and, as one more hypothesis, the open asking worth most), `thinking` (`think` for an expert the kernel is running: askings are yielded to the runner); §76.13 with five Lean lemmas (`taking_the_larger_is_best`, `dearer_worth_no_more`, `mean_had_no_more`, `dearer_has_no_more`, `dearer_puts_out`), built in 13 s; 32 tests of the frame pass. Package (`nca-phonetics` 0.12.0): `tell` runs `thinking` over one `Unsure` concept; the chance is the reading of the core's rung for the input's group (`Grouped`, fitted by `fit_groups`: 81 temperatures, one for each count of stretches and band of level); the record of askings and the temperatures are data (`telling.npz`, 42 kB), fitted in 2 s on the 20,400 choosing-group trials of Catalan and English. The slope 2.0 n + 2.4, the bar 0.9 and the order of asking are gone from the code; the request states the charge (default 0.02). 27 package tests pass in the clean consumer.

### (d) The same frame for an expert of another package, named before measuring. Statement (19:36)

Named: `metrology/sample-settled` of the package `nca-precision-balance` (entry point `nca.precision-balance.settled`; a transducer port to a settled sample: an indication above or below the reference, and a timestamp). The domain is weighing, not sound: is the load on the pan above the reference mass? The question asked while unsure is "sample the transducer again", a request for `metrology.settled-sample` from the port that the kernel serves through that expert. The loads and the noise are the bench's own (mass ±0.8 with ±0.12 spread, a sample's noise 0.96 as its rough indication has). What decides is the installed core unchanged: `Askings`, `Worth`, `Unsure` and `think` with the kernel in hand (the telling expert uses the same three through `thinking`, from inside a request; here the asker stands outside the expert). The chance of "above" is the reading of core rungs fitted by `fit_groups` on 2,000 loads, the group being how many samples were taken; the record of askings is of those loads sampled seven times each. Scored on 2,000 other loads at the three charges of (c).

Predictions: one sample is right 0.80 of the time (Φ(0.8/0.96)); at the default charge 0.02 the frame takes 1.5 to 3 further samples a load and is right at least 0.90; at equal samples a load it is at least 1.5 points above always taking a fixed odd number and answering by the majority (interpolated between 1, 3, 5 and 7); the stated chance is within 3 points of the share right at each charge; dearer charges ask less and are right no more often. Unreached if the kernel cannot plan the asking through that expert, or if any line of `nca.thinking` has to change for it.

German is promised 3.0 to 3.5 points more than it is paid, Belarusian is paid 1.7 to 3.0 more than it is promised. The two fall together with the charge in all eight: HELD. So the direction and the bound of §76.13 hold everywhere, and the amount is right to about a point in the corpora nearest the fitting languages' difficulty and off by up to 3.5 where a language is harder (German voicing) or easier (Belarusian) than Catalan and English as a whole. What bounds it is the calibration across languages, the same thing that gives Belarusian's 3.3 with one stretch: the worth is made of the stated chances, and they are fitted on two languages.

### Idea 3, no fit beyond temperatures: calibrate on the choosing groups of all five taught languages. Statement (19:40)

What the one scoring showed: the stated chance is fitted on two languages and is off where a language is easier or harder than those two as a whole (Belarusian underconfident by 3.3 with one stretch, German overconfident by 2.6; the record's promise off by up to 3.5 in German). Theorem 37.1 carries calibration to mixtures of the groups fitted on, and Catalan with English is a narrow mixture. The choosing group (group 0) exists in all five taught languages and was kept out of every listener's fit; only its Catalan and English trials were ever collected. Test: collect the choosing-group trials of French, Belarusian and German as well (about 90 s each, no fitting), fit temperatures and record on the five together (seconds), and score the reserved speakers and the three test corpora once more: this is a new candidate, scored once.

Predictions: with one stretch of each the error falls to at most 2.5 in Belarusian (from 3.3) and German (from 2.6), and no corpus is above 3 either with one stretch or at the default charge; the largest gap between what the record promises and what is paid falls from 3.5 to at most 2.5; at 0.11.0's questions the share right stays within 0.3 points of the two-language fit in every corpus. Dropped if Belarusian's one-stretch error falls by under 0.4 (half of the 0.8 predicted) or any corpus goes over 3 that was under.

## Asking learned and used across experts (night goal, taken up 20:22)

The user sent the next goal as a message before going to bed (three items: a chance that reads what is being decided; a record learned from the system's own askings; one asker over several experts), with the rule that nothing waits for them. Decisions of mine so far, each the conservative one: the goal is worked on although it did not arrive as the app's goal command; the machine is held awake for the session; at most six processes, so tiny tests wait as long as the six from-sound runs above are going.

A fresh speaker group. Every group but 0 (choosing) and 19 (reserved) was heard by the shipped listeners and by the stand-ins, and group 19 has now been scored three times (0.11's two readings, 0.12, the five-language fit). So no group is fresh as things stand. Group 18 is set aside from now: nothing is chosen on it and nothing more is fitted on it. Since the listeners heard it, a candidate is scored on it only with stand-in listeners refitted without groups 0, 18 and 19 (two fits of 240 s on this machine), and that is done once a candidate has passed its test on the choosing group, not before.

### Item 1, idea 1: the group reads how far apart the two kinds are in the voice that said both. Statement (20:22)

What bounded calibration tonight: the chance reads counts of stretches and the level of the distances between the fresh sound and the two kinds, and not what is being told apart; German voicing is right 0.76 of the time however sure the lead looks. The request holds more than it uses. Both kinds come from one voice, so how unlike that voice's own tokens of the first kind are to its tokens of the second is there to be measured, with no name and no second voice in it: the separation s = the mean warped distance between a token of one kind and a token of the other. Where a speaker barely keeps two sounds apart s is small, and a lead between the fresh sound and two nearly alike references says little. Which way the temperature should move with s is for the fit; the claim is only that s carries what the language and the contrast did, and by Theorem 37.1 groups that make the languages alike carry across them.

Test, no fit beyond temperatures: redraw the choosing-group trials of Catalan and English (the drawing is seeded), measure s for the first three tokens of each kind, and run the four cells of this evening (fitted on one language and run on the other, and half against half) with the group = (counts, band of level, band of s), three bands each. Predictions: across languages the error with one stretch of each falls from 2.8 and 2.4 to at most 2.0 in both; the final answers' error at the default charge is at most 1.3 in all four cells (now 0.6 to 1.3); at 0.11.0's questions the share right is at least 0.2 points above tonight's grouping on the mean of the four. Dropped if the mean of the two cross-language one-stretch errors falls by under 0.3 (half of the 0.6 predicted). Cost to a request if kept: one more batch of warping, about a third more time.
