### Item 1, idea 2: the group reads the kind of contrast, as the listeners name the two kinds. Probe with the true kind first. Statement (20:30)

A request's rows are the roots of the two listeners' naming chances side by side, so what each kind is named is in the request: the sounds the listeners give most chance over a kind's stretches, and from the two kinds' names whether they differ in voicing, place, manner or as vowels. Building that namer is an hour; whether it could help is a test of seconds, because the stored trials carry the true kind of contrast. So first the upper bound: the four cells with the group = (counts, band of level, true family of the contrast). No reading of names can do better than the true family does. Prediction: across languages the mean one-stretch error falls from 2.63 to at most 1.8. If it falls by under 0.4 the idea is dropped without building the namer and counts as the second failure. Beside it, to see where the gap lives: the slope fitted for each family in each language, with one stretch of each.

### Item 1, idea 2. Outcome of the probe (20:31): FAILED, dropped

With the true family of the contrast in the group the one-stretch error across languages is 2.52 (fitted on Catalan, run on English) and 3.53 (the other way): mean 3.03 against 2.63 without it. It did not fall at all, so no reading of names can make this group work and the namer is not built. Second failed idea of item 1. Why, from the slopes fitted with one stretch of each (the k of 1 / (1 + exp(−k · lead))) and the share right:

| | Catalan: share right, slope | English: share right, slope |
|---|---|---|
| manner | 0.929, 8.75 | 0.878, 5.75 |
| place | 0.889, 6.25 | 0.858, 7.75 |
| voicing | 0.873, 6.0 | 0.721, 3.0 |
| vowel | 0.857, 6.0 | 0.747, 4.75 |

The same family has a different slope in the two languages, and not in one direction (place is steeper in English, the others shallower). What the lead is worth turns on the language and the family together: English voicing keeps nearly Catalan's leads (0.35 against 0.41 in the mean) and is right 0.72 of the time against 0.87. A lead that is as large and wrong more often is a fresh voice lying nearer the wrong kind for a reason of its own, and neither the family nor the three distances of a request (fresh to each kind, kind to kind) say so.

### Item 1, idea 3: the group reads whether the two listeners, each taken alone, choose the same kind. Statement (20:31)

Searched after two failures. What the literature holds for a stated chance that must survive a shift it was not fitted on: scores from the agreement of differently built models track the error under shift where a single model's confidence, however scaled on the source, overstates it (the benchmarks of error prediction under shift; Jiang et al. on disagreement equalling error for calibrated ensembles; Ovadia et al. on ensembles keeping calibration under shift), with the caveat that models can agree and be wrong together. A request already holds two listeners of different build side by side in every row, so each one's own distances, and with them each one's own choice, can be had from the same rows at the price of two more batches of warping.

Theory: a fresh voice lying nearer the wrong kind for a reason of its own (the outcome above) is a place where what was learned does not hold, and two differently built listeners need not fail there alike; where they choose differently the joint lead is worth less whatever its size. Test in seconds, since the choosing-group trials of Catalan and English were also collected with each listener alone: the four cells with the group = (counts, band of level, the two listeners agree or not at that count). Predictions: across languages the mean one-stretch error falls from 2.63 to at most 2.0; the final answers' error at the default charge is at most 1.3 in all four cells; no loss at 0.11.0's questions on the mean of the four (at least −0.1). Dropped if the cross-language mean falls by under 0.3. A third failure closes item 1.

### Item 1, idea 3. Outcome (20:32): FAILED. Item 1 is closed by its stop rule: UNREACHED

| cell | one stretch, error: shipped grouping | with agreement | final answers at 0.02: shipped | with agreement | against 0.11.0 at its questions: shipped | with agreement |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| fitted on Catalan, run on English | 2.82 | 2.84 | 1.29 | 1.52 | +0.96 | +0.93 |
| fitted on English, run on Catalan | 2.43 | 2.74 | 0.76 | 0.74 | +0.35 | +0.31 |
| Catalan, half against half | 0.53 | 0.67 | 0.58 | 0.47 | +0.40 | +0.34 |
| English, half against half | 1.18 | 0.99 | 0.99 | 1.25 | +0.73 | +0.56 |

Across languages the mean one-stretch error is 2.79 against 2.63: FAILED. At most 1.3 at the default in all four: FAILED (1.52). No loss at 0.11.0's questions: HELD (−0.08 on the mean). The listeners agree on 0.863 of Catalan trials and 0.813 of English ones and agreement does tell right from wrong within a language (Catalan 0.922 right where they agree, 0.651 where they differ; English 0.862 and 0.604). But where they agree English is still right 6 points less often than Catalan, with the shallower slope (5.25 against 6.5): the two listeners are wrong together, which is the caveat the literature states.

Three ideas in a row failed their predictions, so item 1 is closed: UNREACHED. No candidate passed its choosing-group test, so nothing was scored on group 18; it stays set aside and unspent. Nothing changes in the package: 0.12.0 stands, with Belarusian 3.3 points under with one stretch and the record's promise within 2 points in 16 of 24 cells.

What bounds it. Three readings of a single request were tried as groups: how far apart the two kinds are in the reference voice, the true family of the contrast (an upper bound on any naming of it), and whether two differently built listeners agree. Each sorts trials within a language and none removes the gap between languages, which has opposite signs in the two directions. So at equal readings of one request a trial of one language is right more often than a trial of another; what differs is a property of the language, or of the listeners' acquaintance with it, that one request does not show. A stated chance that holds in a language it was not fitted on then has to draw on more than one request. One source needs no names: by 76.9 a calibrated chance of the answer held does not move in the mean when more is heard, so over a run of requests the system's own askings show whether its chances are too sure (they fall after an asking) or too modest (they rise). That is a reading of the record of one's own askings and belongs with item 2; it is noted there and was not tried under item 1.

## Item 2: a record learned from the system's own askings

Theory first: §76.14 is written (a try costs at most the charge, m K δ in all; a rule that reads only place and band makes askings drawn from the same law as all askings, so its own record has the full record's means where it goes), with `NCA.try_costs_charge` and `NCA.tries_cost_at_most` in Lean (the file builds, 10 s). In the core: `Learning(askings, charge, tries, every)` holds a record that grows, and `Unsure` given one records each asking served with how the inquiry stood before and after, and offers an asking recorded fewer than `tries` times at its place and band with the chance one, so the frame makes it. No names enter: the record is of chances before and after, so it can be learned where nobody says what was right. On the core's two-answer inquiry of the tests (readings right 0.7 of the time, charge 0.02, 12 tries) the record begun empty pays on fresh trials within a point of the record of every asking after 3,000 trials; 34 tests pass.

### Item 2, idea 1: plain tries on a coarse record. Statement (20:34)

Stated exploration: m tries for each asking at each place and band, the one recorded least first, nothing else. The cost of learning is the number of cells: telling sounds apart has 26 places with an asking open, three askings, and with the shipped 50 bands some 25 bands above a half, near 2,000 cells; with 10 bands about 350, of which the common ones fill within a few hundred trials. So the record is begun coarse. Run through the core's frame unchanged (`think`, `Unsure`, `Learning`) on stored distances: the choosing-group trials of Catalan and English with the shipped listeners (20,400), chances as the shipped teller reads them, shuffled once; one half is the stream learned from, in order, the other half is fresh. Paid = right × (1 − charge)^askings, charge 0.02. The full record is the record of every asking of the stream half, at the same bands.

Predictions, 10 bands and m = 10: (1) the full record at 10 bands pays within 0.3 points of the full record at 50 bands on the fresh half; (2) the record of one's own askings, frozen after n trials and run without tries on the fresh half, pays within 1 point of the full record from n = 1,000 on; (3) while learning, tries included, the mean paid over a window of 500 trials is within 1 point from trial 3,000 on; (4) all tries together cost under m K δ = 10 × 350 × 0.02 = 70 trials' worth, and the record ends with under a third of the full record's askings. Dropped if (2) needs more than 2,000 trials. Weighing is stated once this has run.

(3) while learning within 1 point from trial 3,000: HELD in the mean from trial 500; single windows of 500 cannot show 1 point (one standard error of the paired difference is about 0.6). As foreseen the cells set the pace: 50 bands need 2,000 trials for what 10 bands do in 200 to 500, and three tries a cell is no faster than ten and ends lower. Asking falls as the record grows (1.69 askings a trial frozen at 200, 1.27 at the end, the full record 1.46) at the same paid, which is 76.2: near the tie between asking and answering a misread worth costs little, and a cell once read as not worth asking is not asked from again, so it is never mended. That stays inside a point here; an asking tried again now and then would mend it and was not needed.

### Item 2, a probe: do one's own askings show whether the stated chance is right, with no names? Statement (20:40)

76.14 (ii) presumes calibrated chances, and item 1 closed on a stated chance that is off in a language it was not fitted on, by an amount no single request shows. A record of one's own askings holds a test of that presumption that needs nobody to say what was right. If chances are calibrated, the chance of the answer held is in the mean the same after an asking as before (the proof of 76.14 uses it). If instead the true chance is the stated one with its odds taken to a power 1/t (t above one: too sure; below one: too modest), then stated chances drift: above a half a too-sure chance is a concave reading of the true one and falls in the mean after an asking, a too-modest one rises. And the t under which the corrected chance stops drifting, the root of mean[g_t(after) − g_t(before)] = 0 with g_t(c) = σ(logit(c) / t), is an estimate of the language's t from askings alone.

Probe in seconds on the stored counts of the eight corpora (reserved speakers with the stand-in listeners and their fit; Spanish, Italian and LibriSpeech with the shipped; no names are used to find t, only to score it): from one stretch of each, the chance of the answer held before and after one more stretch of each of the three kinds. Predictions: (1) the sign of the mean drift is opposite to the sign of stated less right in at least 7 of 8 corpora; (2) the root t is below one for Belarusian and above one for German; (3) reading the one-stretch chance through g_t with each corpus's own root brings |stated less right| to at most 2.0 in Belarusian (now 3.3) and German (2.6) and worsens no corpus by more than 0.5. Dropped if (1) holds in fewer than 6 of 8. This is a probe of what item 2's record can carry; it does not reopen item 1, changes no package, and nothing is scored on group 18.

### Item 2, the probe. Outcome (20:41): two of three predictions held; the finding stands and is taken up after item 3

Every trial of each corpus, one stretch of each, then one more of the first kind, of the second or of the fresh sound (5,520 to 16,200 trials a corpus; under 10 s each). Points; t is the power the odds are taken to.

| corpus | stated less right | drift of the held answer's chance after an asking | root t, from askings alone | the t names would choose | stated less right, read through the root |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Spanish | −0.75 | +0.39 | 0.85 | 1.02 | +1.38 |
| Italian | −0.88 | +0.48 | 0.84 | 0.97 | +1.41 |
| LibriSpeech | −2.22 | +0.90 | 0.74 | 0.84 | +1.69 |
| Catalan, reserved | +0.13 | +0.10 | 0.96 | 1.02 | +0.57 |
| English, reserved | +1.05 | −0.41 | 1.18 | 1.10 | −1.26 |
| French, reserved | −1.95 | +0.42 | 0.84 | 0.87 | +0.06 |
| Belarusian, reserved | −3.27 | +0.58 | 0.78 | 0.79 | −0.56 |
| German, reserved | +2.61 | −0.77 | 1.38 | 1.21 | −2.02 |

(1) Drift opposite in sign to stated less right in at least 7 of 8: HELD, 7 of 8; the eighth is Catalan, where both are a tenth of a point. (2) Root below one for Belarusian, above one for German: HELD, and the Belarusian root (0.78) is the t its names would choose (0.79). (3) Read through its own root, Belarusian at most 2.0: HELD (0.56); German at most 2.0: FAILED by a hair (2.02, now on the other side); no corpus worse by more than 0.5: FAILED (Spanish +0.63, Italian +0.53). The root overshoots: in seven of eight corpora it lies further from one than the t names would choose, on the same side. As it stands, with no names from the language at hand, the largest |stated less right| over the eight corpora falls from 3.27 to 2.02.

So the askings a system makes for its own ends also tell it, in a language nobody labelled for it, whether it is too sure or too modest, and by roughly how much. No single request shows that (item 1's three failures); a run of requests does. Why the root overshoots is not known; one reading is that the presumed form (one power for every count of stretches) is too simple, since the three askings drift by different amounts. Decision, mine and the conservative one: item 1 stays closed as its stop rule has it and is reported unreached; item 3 comes next, as the order says; this is taken up after it as a second idea of item 2 (the record learning how sure to be, beside what asking is worth), with a statement and prediction of its own before anything is built, chosen on the choosing group only.

## Item 3: one asker over several experts

Theory first: §76.15 (each asking its own charge δ_a; one rule over all askings has the most that any way of answering and asking has; more askings open leave no less to be had; one asking made dearer leaves no more and is taken nowhere new; a try costs at most its own charge), with `NCA.more_askings_have_no_less` and `NCA.one_dearer_has_no_more` in Lean. In the core a `Worth` and a `Learning` take one charge or each asking's own; nothing else changed, and `Unsure` is as it was. On the core's own two-answer inquiry, held outside any expert with a further reading (right 0.7, charge 0.02) served by one expert and a close reading (right 0.95, dearer) by another in one kernel, the one asker is paid no less than an asker over either alone and asks for the close reading less at each dearer charge. Core `1f7bf2f6` (2,279 passed, 8 skipped; the asking tests now in `tests/runtime/test_asking.py`); this is the core every package below is measured with, and it is not changed between them.

### Item 3. The three packages, named before measuring, and what is predicted (20:47)

1. `nca-precision-balance` (metrology): is the load above or below the reference. Askings: a settled sample again, served by the installed `metrology/sample-settled`, charge 0.02; a dwell, the middle of five samples, served by a second expert, charge 0.06; a question to a person who compares against the reference and is right 0.99 of the time, served by a third, charge 0.12.
2. `nca-equipment-diagnosis` (fault finding, written by the other agent and untouched by me so far): which of four assemblies failed. Askings at the fees the machine itself states: the startup spectrum, which points to a channel (0.035, to be had only before any instrument is read), and an instrument reading on a channel (0.065 each), served by two experts over the package's `Machine`.
3. `nca-phonetics` (speech): which of two kinds a fresh sound is. Askings: hear the first kind, the second or the fresh sound again (0.02 each, served by the speaker and `name-in`), and a question to a person who says which kind it is and is right 0.98 of the time (0.15).

In each the inquiry is held by the evaluation, outside every expert; the chances are calibrated by the core's rungs on practice trials with names; the record of askings is begun empty and learned by `Learning` from the rule's own askings (10 tries), then frozen and scored on fresh trials. Paid = a right answer less each asking's own charge, as shares.

Predictions, for each package: (P1) the one asker is paid no less than the asker left only one kind of asking, whichever kind, to within 0.5 points; (P2) in at least two of the three it is paid at least 1 point more than the best of those; (P3) with the dearest asking it uses made three times as dear and nothing else changed, that asking is made at least a third less often and the others no less often; (P4) the final answers' stated chance is within 3 points of the share right over ten bands; (P5) a decision takes under 1 ms beside what the experts themselves take. Item 3 is met if P1 and P3 hold in all three with the one core; a package where either fails is a failed idea of this item.

So 76.15 (iii) holds (no more often at any dearer fee, and the stop where the worths cross) and my number was wrong: how fast an asking is given up turns on how far its worth stands above the answer's chance, which here is far. ### Item 2, idea 2: the record also learns how sure to be. Statement for the choosing-group test (20:59)

Written while the from-sound runs of item 3 are going; run once they are done. Theory as in the probe: if in the setting at hand the true chance of the answer held is g_t(c) = σ(logit(c) / t) of the stated c, then g_t of the stated chance does not drift over an asking, and the root t̂ of mean[g_s(after) − g_s(before)] = 0 over one's own askings estimates t with no names. The probe found the root on the right side in the corpora that matter and too far out in seven of eight, which is what one power for every count of stretches gives where the later counts are less off than the first. So the power used is t̂^λ with one shrink λ for every language, and λ has to be chosen where names may be used to choose: on the choosing groups (Catalan, English, French and Belarusian with the shipped listeners, German with the stand-ins), never on the reserved speakers or on Spanish, Italian and LibriSpeech, whose figures in the probe I have seen for λ = 1 and do not use.

Test in seconds: on the five choosing-group corpora, t̂ from the three askings open after one stretch of each, and the one-stretch error (ten bands) of the chance read through g of t̂^λ for λ in 0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1 (λ = 0 is the shipped chance). Predictions: (1) the root lies on the side of one that names would choose in every corpus where names would choose a t more than 0.05 from one; (2) the λ with the smallest worst error over the five is 0.5 or 0.75; (3) at that λ the worst one-stretch error over the five is at most 2.0 and under what λ = 0 gives. Dropped if the best λ is 0 (the correction does not help where it is chosen). If it passes, λ is frozen and the candidate goes on: a core form, then one scoring.

### Item 2, idea 2: the core form, and the statement for its scoring (21:04)

In the core (not yet committed): `Sureness` keeps, for each asking served, the stated chance of the answer held before and of that same answer after, and gives the power nearest one under which they stop drifting (§76.16, with `NCA.right_chance_does_not_drift`, `NCA.too_sure_falls` and `NCA.too_modest_rises` in Lean); a `Learning` that holds one values its record by the chances so read, and `Unsure` gives the answers at those chances while keeping the record by the chances as stated. On the core's own inquiry made to take its readings for right 0.8 of the time when they are right 0.7 of the time (and 0.62, and 0.7), the power found from its own askings is within 12% of the true one in all three and the final answers' stated chance comes within 2 points of the share right where it was 4 or more off.

Scoring on speech, in seconds, through `think`, `Unsure`, `Learning` and `Sureness` unchanged, λ = 0.75 as frozen: each of the eight corpora is a setting of its own. Its trials are shuffled; the rule runs through all of them in order at the default charge with the listeners' own record of askings (no tries), learning the power from the askings it makes of its own accord, with no names; the second half is scored, beside the same rule without `Sureness`. The power within 0.05 of its final value from 800 askings on: FAILED (German 0.93 against 1.13, Italian 1.07 against 0.95, Catalan 0.81 against 1.00). Belarusian under 3.0 from 400 askings and under 2.0 from 1,600: HELD (1.3 from the first 200). No corpus over 3.0 from 400 on: FAILED: German reads 5.3 at 400 and 3.8 at 800, on the wrong side of one until some 1,600 askings, and Italian 4.4 at 400. Where the drift is large (Belarusian) a few hundred askings find it; where it is a few tenths of a point, a few hundred askings give a mean drift whose scatter (the change of a chance over one asking has a spread near 0.2, so 1.4 points over 200 askings) is larger than the drift, and the root follows the scatter. With `least` at 200 the core's `Sureness` therefore does harm in its first thousand askings in some settings. The from-sound chain now running was started with that default and will show it or not; it is left to finish and reported as it comes.

What follows from the theory itself, not from these corpora: a root is to be believed only where the drift it removes is more than chance gives. So `Sureness` should claim a power only when the mean drift at power one stands off from nought by a stated number of its own standard errors, and say one otherwise.

### Item 2, idea 3: a power is claimed only where the drift is more than chance gives. Statement (21:19)

The gate: with n askings followed and d the change of the held answer's stated chance over each, the power is the root only if |mean(d)| is at least two standard errors, sd(d) / √n, and one otherwise. Two is the usual line, set before looking. Test in seconds on the same askings as above. Predictions: no corpus is worse than as shipped by more than 0.5 points at any count of askings; the gate opens for Belarusian within 400 askings and its error is under 3.0 from there on; Catalan and English stay at power one throughout; with all askings every corpus is at most 3.0 unless German's gate stays shut, in which case German stands as shipped (3.08). Dropped if any corpus is worse than shipped by more than 1.0 at any count, since a gate that still lets harm through is no gate.

But a gate lets the whole root through the moment it opens, and the root of a drift that has only just cleared two standard errors is itself uncertain by about as much as it claims.

### Item 2, idea 4: the least correction the askings demand. Statement (21:20)

Under each power t the drift has its own mean and standard error. The power used is the one nearest one under which the mean drift is within two of its standard errors of nought: one itself while the askings do not contradict it, then moving out only as far as they force, and reaching the root as the askings grow. There is no jump when the evidence arrives, and nothing is claimed that two standard errors do not carry. The shrink stays as frozen. Predictions on the same askings: no corpus worse than shipped by more than 0.5 at any count; Belarusian under 3.0 from 800 askings on; with all askings every corpus at most 3.0 and Belarusian at most 2.0. Dropped if any corpus is worse than shipped by more than 0.5 at any count: this is the third try at the same fault, and a third miss closes it.

That is what stands: the first form mends Belarusian from sound and harms German; the form in the core harms nothing and has, from sound, not yet been seen to mend.

The core's `thinking.py` had reached the file-size limit, so asking while unsure moved unchanged into `nca.asking` (`00989b18`; 2,280 tests pass). The packages' imports follow.

### What the learning teller costs a request, and what was shipped (21:39)

One-stretch telling requests through the kernel, each figure from a fresh process of the clean consumer (core `00989b18`, `nca-phonetics` 0.13.0) on a quiet machine, twice each: the installed teller 0.670 and 0.674 ms in the median (0.85 at the 95th percentile, peak 214 and 216 MB); the teller handed a `Sureness` 0.684 and 0.659 ms (215 MB). No difference to be measured. The three packages' evaluations and `own_askings.py` give their saved results to the digit after the core's move. `nca-phonetics` 0.13.0 is `83cb47c`; `nca-precision-balance` `9049f81` and `nca-equipment-diagnosis` `194474d` only follow the imports. Word teaching is untouched by 0.13.0: the installed teller is as in 0.12.0 and the learning one is an option a host takes.

### The careful form from sound where the stored askings say it acts earliest. Statement (21:40)

The Belarusian and German chains ended before the power could leave one. By the stored askings LibriSpeech is the corpus whose drift clears two standard errors earliest (power 0.95 at 400 askings, 0.93 at 800), and its from-sound plan has 318 trials at about 1.9 stretches each, some 600 askings; French on the reserved speakers is next (0.86 at 200 askings, 0.95 at 400, back to 1 at 800: unsteady), with 246 trials and some 330 askings. One run each of the learning teller on those plans, beside the installed teller's answers to the same trials this evening (LibriSpeech: stated less right −2.97 with one stretch, −0.28 at 0.02; French: −1.83 and +0.57). LibriSpeech is a test only and stays one: nothing is fitted with its names, the teller follows its own askings as it would wherever it is used. Predictions: LibriSpeech's power is below one at the end, between 0.90 and 0.99, and its one-stretch stated less right lies between −2.97 and −2.4 (the last third of the trials read a little surer); French's power ends between 0.85 and 1.0; in neither is the power above one at the end, and in both the share right at 0.02 is within 1 point of the installed teller's. If LibriSpeech's power is still one at the end the form has not been seen to act from sound and that stands as the bound.

It is one run of 318 trials; the size of the mending from sound is not established by it (chance alone gives a ten-band gap of 3.5 on so few trials), only its direction and that it does no harm.
